is not a sheaf, since for example on a disconnected scheme
S=S_{1}\sqcup S_{2}
sections of the form

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}|_{S_{1}})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}(S_{1})\text{ and }(0\subseteq\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{E}|_{S_{2}})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}(S_{2})

will trivially agree on the empty overlap, but there cannot exist a section of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{V}}(S)
which restricts to the above two since they have differing lengths. Thus, we consider the sheafification of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
. Consider the presheaf
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
defined above and the sheaf
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
of
2.1
. There is a natural injection of presheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})

which satisfies the universal property of sheafification. In particular, it shows that

(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}.

Proof.

It is clear that we have the injection given above since the condition on the ranks of a section
(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
guarantees that

\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\neq\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}

for all
T\in\Sch_{S}
, and thus the flag is lowered.

To show that this injection satisfies the universal property of sheafification, we must show that any section of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
locally belongs to the image of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Thus, consider a section

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}|_{T})\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}(T)

for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
. Since each
\mathcal{V}_{k}
is finite locally free, we can find a cover
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
over which all
\mathcal{V}_{k}
simultaneously become of constant rank. However, it is then clear that each restriction

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}|_{T})|_{T_{i}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{T_{i}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{p_{i}}|_{T_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}|_{T_{i}})

is a flag of constant rank submodules and thus belongs to the image of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}(T_{i})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}(T_{i})
. Hence,
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
as claimed and we are done.
∎

Remark 2.7
.

Instead of choosing to truncate our lowered flags and paying the associated costs, one can instead do the following. Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
. In light of
2.3
, any truncated lowered flag has length
\ell<d
, and so they can all be uniquely extended to a lowered flag of length exactly
d-1
. Thus, defining the functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sch_{S}

\displaystyle\to\Sets

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\{0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{d-1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}|_{T}\mid\text{the flag is lowered}\}

one obtains a clear isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{E}).

Working with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}}
, the proof that it is a sheaf is simpler than the proof given for
2.5
since we do not need to track changing lengths of flags. However, the map
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}}
can no longer be seen as a literal subsheaf, and instead also requires the extension of flags on the right. Later on, we will define the type morphisms for lowered flags, which will be a map
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}
based on the ranks of the components. If one does this naively with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}}
, the resulting tuples of the form
(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{\ell},d,d,\ldots,d)
do not occur in
\mathcal{P}_{n}
, so truncation would be required at this point, or a discussion of the largest component of the flag not equal to
\mathcal{E}
, or one could replace
\mathcal{P}_{n}
with a sheaf of lowered tuples, etc. For these later applications, we choose to include the truncation in the definition of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
.

\blacksquare

Remark 2.8
.

One may also work with the symmetric notion of
raised flags
, where a flag

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}

is raised if for all
T\in\Sch_{S}
and
i
such that
\mathcal{V}_{i-1}|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}
, then
\mathcal{V}_{i-1}|_{T}=0=\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}
. In this case, raised flags truncate on the left after restrictions and thus it is more natural to index flags in the reverse direction, i.e., as

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell-1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}.

Raised flags similarly form a sheaf, which we denote
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Flags in
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
are also raised flags, and so when
\mathcal{E}
is of constant rank
d
there is an analogue of
2.6
saying that the injection

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}

has the universal property of sheafification and so
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}
as well. Of course, this means we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-0.86108pt\ell\hskip-0.75348pt\textit{a\hskip-0.5382ptg}}}

where
\varphi_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}
is a uniquely defined isomorphism of sheaves. Because this (or a similar) raising isomorphism will be of use later, we take a moment here to detail how this isomorphism behaves.

Let
(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}(S)
be a global section. Since vector bundles on schemes are Zariski locally of constant rank, we can find a Zariski cover
S=\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
over which all
\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i}}
are constant rank. Since the flag is lowered, for each
i\in I
the restriction appears as

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U_{i}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{k_{i}}|_{U_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}|_{U_{i}}

for some integer
k_{i}\leq\ell
, and

\rank_{\mathcal{O}|_{U_{i}}}(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i}})<\rank_{\mathcal{O}|_{U_{i}}}(\mathcal{V}_{j+1}|_{U_{i}})

for all
0\leq j\leq k_{i}
. This means that for any further restriction, i.e. along any
T\to U_{i}
, the length of the restricted flag will remain
k_{i}
. Thus, we see that the length of the flag is also locally constant and furthermore is constant over each
U_{i}
. Therefore, we may group the
U_{i}
together by length of the restriction, defining
W_{j}=\bigcup_{i\in I\atop k_{i}=j}U_{i}
, to obtain a finite disjoint Zariski cover

S=W_{j_{1}}\sqcup W_{j_{2}}\sqcup\ldots\sqcup W_{j_{m}}\sqcup W_{\ell}

for a subset
\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}\}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,\ell-1\}
. By construction, the restriction of the flag to
W_{j_{i}}
has length
j_{i}
. Any global submodule
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{E}
can then be written as a tuple of its restrictions to these
W_{j_{i}}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{V}=(\mathcal{V}|_{W_{j_{1}}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}|_{W_{j_{m}}},\mathcal{V}|_{W_{\ell}}).

Doing this for the components of the flag (and writing it vertically), we see that we obtain a staircase pattern

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0)}

above which we simply have restrictions of
\mathcal{E}
and below which we have proper submodules. The raising isomorphism
\varphi_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}
then slides the non-trivial part of the flag over each component to the top, augmenting with zeros below. That is, it produces the flag

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1-{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}+1-j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}+1-j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-j_{1}}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1-{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}+1-j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-j_{2}}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1-{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-j_{m}}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{\ell}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0)}

with the zeroes below the (horizontally reflected) staircase pattern and non-zero submodules above. Note that the
W_{\ell}
column is fixed and otherwise the order of the modules in each column is preserved.

\blacksquare

Lemma 2.9
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
. The natural action of
\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}
on
\mathcal{E}
induces an action on
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
. For
B\in\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}
, it behaves as

B\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})=(0\subseteq B\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq B\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}).

Proof.

Whenever we have
\mathcal{E}|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\oplus\mathcal{W}
for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
and
1\leq i\leq\ell
, then we also have
\mathcal{E}|_{T}=B|_{T}\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\oplus B|_{T}\mathcal{W}
, so this action preserves the property of being locally a direct summand. Thus, we only need to check that the resulting section is still a lowered flag, then it is obvious that this is the group action induced as claimed. However, since
B
is invertible, if for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
we have

(B\mathcal{V}_{i})|_{T}=(B\mathcal{V}_{i+1})|_{T},

then by applying
B^{-1}|_{T}
we have that
\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}
. This implies that
\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=\mathcal{E}|_{T}\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}
, and so reapplying
B|_{T}
yields

(B\mathcal{V}_{i})|_{T}=\mathcal{E}|_{T}=(B\mathcal{V}_{i+1})|_{T}.

Thus, the resulting flag is also lowered and we are done.
∎

2.B.
Generalized Severi-Brauer Sheaves

When working with an Azumaya algebra of degree
d
, the Severi-Brauer scheme can be defined as the scheme representing the sheaf of right ideals of rank
d
, i.e., of reduced rank
1
(see
Section

5.A
). The word generalized means that we instead allow flags of right ideals, and therefore to obtain a sheaf with must consider lowered flags of right ideals.

Definition 2.10
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d=n+1
. Define
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
to be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
consisting of lowered flags

0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{A}

where each
\mathcal{I}_{j}
is a right ideal of
\mathcal{A}
. Each
\mathcal{I}_{j}
must also locally be a direct summand of
\mathcal{A}
, as this condition is imposed by
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Symmetrically, define
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}
to be the subsheaf of raised flags of right ideals in
\mathcal{A}
.

Since the only additional condition imposed on the modules in a flag, namely being a right ideal, can be checked locally, it is clear that
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
is in fact a sheaf. For a flag of right ideals
(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{A})\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(S)
, each
\mathcal{I}_{j}
is a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module which is locally a direct summand of
\mathcal{A}
since this condition is imposed by
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
. However, the property of being a right ideal of
\mathcal{A}
imposes a further restriction on the rank of
\mathcal{I}_{j}
. In particular,
\rank(\mathcal{I}_{j})
is divisible by
d
, i.e., for any
T\in\Sch_{S}
for which
\mathcal{I}|_{T}
is constant rank,
\rank(\mathcal{I}|_{T})=dr_{j}
for some
0\leq r_{j}\leq d
. This can be seen by localizing sufficiently far such that
\mathcal{A}
becomes a matrix algebra where the description of direct summand right ideals is analogous to the case over a field.

Remark 2.11
.

Within a separable
\mathcal{O}
–algebra, i.e., an algebra which is locally isomorphic to a finite direct product of Azumaya algebras, we can equally well discuss flags of right ideals. This will come up later when we consider a degree
2
étale cover
f\colon L\to S
and an Azumaya algebra
\mathcal{B}
over
L
. Then, the pushforward
f_{*}(\mathcal{B})
is a separable
\mathcal{O}
–algebra which is not an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra, but nevertheless we can discuss
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{B})}
and
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{B})}
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 2.12
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. For any section

(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{A})\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(S)

we have
\ell<d
.

Proof.

Since the given flag of right ideals is also a section of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(S)
, we know that
\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\neq\mathcal{A}
. Now, assume
\ell\geq d
. Consider a cover
\{U_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
over which all
\mathcal{I}_{j}
become of constant rank. Each flag

0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}|_{U_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{2}|_{U_{i}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}|_{U_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}|_{U_{i}}

is a lowered flag of right ideals of constant rank, and so
\rank(\mathcal{I}_{j}|_{U_{i}})\geq\min(dj,d^{2})
since any potential equalities between components can only occur on the right. Thus, if
\ell\geq d
, then
\rank(\mathcal{I}_{\ell}|_{U_{i}})=d^{2}
which means that
\mathcal{I}_{\ell}|_{U_{i}}=\mathcal{A}|_{U_{i}}
for all
i\in I
. Of course, this means that
\mathcal{I}_{\ell}=\mathcal{A}
globally, giving a contradiction. Thus,
\ell<d
as claimed.
∎

It will also be helpful to view
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
as the sheafification of a particular presheaf. We define

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}\subset\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{A}}

to be the subfunctor of flags consisting of constant rank, locally direct summand submodules which are also right ideals.

Lemma 2.13
.

There is a commutative diagram of presheaves

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}}

where the injection
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
has the univerisal property of sheafification and so
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proof.

The commutativity of the diagram is clear from the definitions of the presheaves and sheaves. For any
T\in\Sch_{S}
and any section

(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{A}|_{T})\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}|_{\mathcal{A}}(T),

we may view it as a section in
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(T)
where we know that it locally belongs to the image of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{A}}
by
2.6
. However, the restrictions of each
\mathcal{I}_{j}
are still right ideals, and therefore the section locally belongs to the image of

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}.

Then, by commutativity of the diagram and the fact that all morphisms are injective, the given section in
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}|_{\mathcal{A}}(T)
locally belongs to the image of

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}

and so this map has the universal property of sheafification, as claimed.
∎

Corollary 2.14
.

There is a commutative diagram of presheaves

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}}

where the injection
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}
has the univerisal property of sheafification and so
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Therefore, we also have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}

where
\varphi_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}
is a unique sheaf isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows by symmetry from the proof
2.13
by using
2.8
in place of
2.6
, and then the universal property of sheafification produces the isomorphism
\varphi_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}
.
∎

The generalized Severi-Brauer sheaf also comes with a natural action of
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
.

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. The natural action of
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{A}
by conjugation induces an action on
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
. For
a\in\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
, it behaves as

a\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{A})=(0\subseteq a\mathcal{I}_{1}a^{-1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq a\mathcal{I}_{\ell}a^{-1}\subseteq\mathcal{A}).

Equivalently, since the
\mathcal{I}_{k}
are right ideals and so
a\mathcal{I}_{k}a^{-1}=a\mathcal{I}_{k}
, this is the left action of
a
by multiplication.

Proof.

Since the conjugation action on right ideals is equivalent to the left multiplication action, we can view the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
as the restriction of the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{A})^{\times}
on
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
where
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{A})^{\times}
as left multiplication operators. From this point of view, the fact that this action is well defined follows immediately from
2.9
.
∎

Now, let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
and consider the Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})
. For a submodule
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
which is locally a direct summand, we define a right ideal of
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})
by setting

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}}=\{\varphi\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})\mid\Img(\varphi)\subseteq\mathcal{V}\},

which is isomorphic to
\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{V})
as an
\mathcal{O}
–module. Note that here,
\Img(\varphi)
is the sheafified image sheaf. Conversely, given a right ideal
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})
which is locally a direct summand, we define a submodule of
\mathcal{E}
via an intersection of submodules

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}=\bigcap\{\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\mid\Img(\varphi)\subseteq\mathcal{W}\text{ for all }\varphi\in\mathcal{I}\},

i.e., this is the minimal submodule of
\mathcal{E}
which contains all images of map in
\mathcal{I}
. Equivalently, we could define a subpresheaf
\bigcup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{I}}\Img(\varphi)\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
of
\mathcal{E}
by

T\mapsto\left(\bigcup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{I}(T)}\Img(\varphi(T))\right)\subseteq\mathcal{E}(T)

which contains all the presheaf images of
\varphi\in\mathcal{I}
, and then

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}=\left(\bigcup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{I}}\Img(\varphi)\right)^{\sharp}

is the sheafification.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank d. Let
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
be a submodule which is locally a direct summand and let
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})
be a right ideal which is locally a direct summand. First,

(i)

the right ideal
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}}
is locally a direct summand of
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})
, and

(ii)

the submodule
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}
is locally a direct summand of
\mathcal{E}
.

Secondly, the two constructions are mutually inverse. In particular

(iii)

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}}}=\mathcal{V}
, and

(iv)

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}}=\mathcal{I}
.

Finally, the constructions are order preserving with respect to inclusion.

(v)

If
\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
are locally direct summands, then
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}
, and

(vi)

If
\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})
are locally direct summand right ideals, then
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}
.

Proof.

Since we only care if the modules are direct sums locally, we may assume we have sufficiently localized and simply consider the case when they are already direct sums.

(i)
: Assume
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W}
for some other submodule
\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
. Then,

\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{V})\oplus\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W})\oplus\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})\oplus\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{W})

and
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{V})=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{V})\oplus\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{V})
is clearly a direct summand of the above expression.

(ii)
: Here, we may further localize until
\mathcal{E}\cong\mathcal{O}^{d}
and thus
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})\cong\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
. In this case, if over some
T\in\Sch_{S}
we have a matrix

\begin{bmatrix}c_{1}&c_{2}&\ldots&c_{d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{I}(T)

where
c_{i}\in\mathcal{O}^{d}(T)
are column vectors, then because
\mathcal{I}
is a right ideal we also have that

\begin{bmatrix}c_{i}&0&\ldots&0\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{I}(T)

for any
i
. Thus, one sees that

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)=\{c\in\mathcal{O}^{d}(T)\mid\begin{bmatrix}c&0&\ldots&0\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{I}(T)\}.

Now, by assumption
\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})=\mathcal{I}\oplus\mathcal{N}
for some
\mathcal{O}
–module
\mathcal{N}
, which is not necessarily also an ideal. So, for any
v\in\mathcal{O}^{d}(T)
we obtain a unique decomposition

\begin{bmatrix}v&0&\ldots 0\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}c_{v}&c_{2}&\ldots&c_{d}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}w_{v}&-c_{2}&\ldots&-c_{d}\end{bmatrix}

with
\begin{bmatrix}c_{v}&c_{2}&\ldots&c_{d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{I}(T)
and
\begin{bmatrix}w_{v}&-c_{2}&\ldots&-c_{d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{N}(T)
. In particular, this means that
c_{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)
and
w_{v}\notin\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)
if
w_{v}\neq 0
. Now, we can set

\mathcal{W}(T)=\{w_{v}\in\mathcal{O}^{d}(T)\mid v\in\mathcal{V}(T)\}

to obtain a submodule
\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
for which it is clear that
\mathcal{O}^{d}=\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}\oplus\mathcal{W}
. Thus,
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}
is a direct summand as desired.

(iii)
and
(iv)
: Since all objects are sheaves, it again suffices to show that the constructions are mutually inverse sufficiently locally. Thus, in the case when
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{O}^{d}
, the considerations in
(ii)
above show that for
T\in\Sch_{S}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}}(T)

\displaystyle=\{\begin{bmatrix}c_{1}&c_{2}&\ldots&c_{d}\end{bmatrix}\mid c_{i}\in\mathcal{V}(T)\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)

\displaystyle=\{c\in\mathcal{O}^{d}(T)\mid\begin{bmatrix}c&0&\ldots&0\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{I}(T)\}.

Then it is clear that
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}}}=\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}}}=\mathcal{I}
.

(v)
: If
\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}
, then endomorphisms of
\mathcal{E}
which have image contained in
\mathcal{V}_{1}
also have image contained in
\mathcal{V}_{2}
, so
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}
.

(vi)
: If
\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{2}
, then

\{\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\mid\Img(\varphi)\subseteq\mathcal{W}\text{ for all }\varphi\in\mathcal{I}_{2}\}\subseteq\{\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\mid\Img(\varphi)\subseteq\mathcal{W}\text{ for all }\varphi\in\mathcal{I}_{1}\}

and so taking intersections will reverse this inclusion, thus
\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}
.
∎

Corollary 2.17
.

We have an isomorphism of functors
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}
defined by

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})\mapsto

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{\ell}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E}))

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}_{\ell}}\subseteq\mathcal{E})\reflectbox{$\mapsto$}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})).

Furthermore, this isomorphism is equivariant with respect to the
\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}=\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}
actions of
2.9
and
2.15
.

Proof.

The passage from submodules to ideals is bijective and order preserving by
2.16
, thus it is clear that our proposed map is an isomorphism so long as lowered flags are sent to lowered flags of ideals. However, this is also clear from bijectivity. Consider a section

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

and assume that there exists
1\leq i\leq\ell-1
and
T\in\Sch_{S}
such that

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}|_{T}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{i+1}}|_{T}.

By bijectivity, this implies that
\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}
and so
\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=\mathcal{E}|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}
since the original flag is lowered. Thus,

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}|_{T}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})|_{T}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{i+1}}|_{T}

which shows that the flag of right ideals is lowered as well.

To see the equivariance, one can compute that for
a\in\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{(a\mathcal{V}_{i})}

\displaystyle=\{\varphi\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})\mid\Img(\varphi)\subseteq a\mathcal{V}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=\{a\varphi\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})\mid\Img(\varphi)\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=a\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}

\displaystyle=a\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}a^{-1}.

Then, since the map is bijective, this also shows that
\mathcal{V}_{(a\mathcal{I}_{i}a^{-1})}=a\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{I}_{i}}
. Hence, the isomorphism is equivariant as claimed, concluding the proof.
∎

2.C.
Isomorphisms with Parabolic Subgroups

We first consider the case when
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}
for a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module
\mathcal{E}
of constant rank
d
. Given a section

\overline{\mathcal{V}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d})\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}},

we set
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})\subseteq\mathbf{G}
to be the stabilizer of this section under the natural action of
2.9
.

Lemma 2.18
.

The stabilizer
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})
is a parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
is a sheaf, a group which is locally parabolic will also be parabolic globally. Thus, we may localize without loss of generality. In particular, we may assume that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{O}^{d}
and that each
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is a free direct summand of
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}
, and thus also a direct summand of
\mathcal{O}^{d}
. Then, we can choose a basis
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{d}\}
of
\mathcal{O}^{d}
such that

\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{k_{i}}\}

is a basis of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
for an increasing sequence of integers
1\leq k_{1}<k_{2}<\ldots<k_{\ell}<d
. By setting
m_{1}=k_{1}
,
m_{i}=k_{i}-k_{i-1}
for
2\leq i\leq\ell
, and
m_{\ell+1}=d-k_{\ell}
, the increasing sequence of integers produces an ordered partition
m_{1}+m_{2}+\ldots+m_{\ell+1}=d
. Then, writing matrices in
\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
with respect to the basis
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{d}\}
, we see that

\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}B_{1}&*&\ldots&*\\
0&B_{2}&\ldots&*\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\ldots&B_{\ell+1}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbf{GL}_{d}\mid B_{i}\in\mathbf{GL}_{m_{i}}\right\}

with zeroes below the block diagonal and arbitrary entries above the block diagonal. We recognize this as a standard parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, finishing the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.19
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{V}}\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
be a flag of constant rank modules, i.e.,
\overline{\mathcal{V}}\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Under the type map of (
10
) we have

t(\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}}))=(\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell}))^{c}.

Proof.

We localize such that all modules in the flag become free direct summands and then we may refer to the ordered partition
m_{1}+m_{2}+\ldots+m_{\ell+1}=d
occurring in the proof of
2.18
. Since the partial sums of this partition are the
k_{i}=\rank(\mathcal{V}_{i})
by construction, we see that

t(\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}}))=(\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell}))^{c}.

Since the modules in the flag are constant rank by assumption, this local description of the type also applies globally, hence the result.
∎

Corollary 2.20
.

Let
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
be the general linear group of an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra
\mathcal{A}
of constant degree
d
. Given a section

\overline{\mathcal{I}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{A})\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}},

we set
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}})\subseteq\mathbf{G}
to be the stabilizer of this section under the natural action of
2.15
. This stabilizer is a parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{G}
.

Proof.

Again, we may work sufficiently locally and assume that
\mathcal{A}=\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
. Then, using the isomorphsim of
2.17
the flag of ideals comes from a flag of submodules
\overline{\mathcal{V}}\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
. Furthermore, since this isomorphism is equivariant with respect to the actions of
\mathbf{G}
, we have that
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}})=\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})
. We then know this is a parabolic subgroup by
2.18
.
∎

Based on the results of
2.18
and
2.20
, we now have maps

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}}).

which turn out to be isomorphisms.

Proposition 2.21
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
and set
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}=\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}
. The two maps
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
and
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
, which send a flag to its stabilizer, are both isomorphisms. Furthermore, they fit into the commutative diagram of isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}}

where the top horizontal map is the isomorphism of
2.17
.

Proof.

It is clear that the diagram is commutative since the isomorphism of
2.17
is
\mathbf{G}
–equivariant. Thus, it is sufficient to show that
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
is an isomorphism.

Consider two sections of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle=(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle=(0\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{E})

and assume
\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{V})=\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{W})
. Since the flags will be equal if and only if they are equal locally, we may localize until all of the occurring modules are free direct summands of
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{O}^{d}
. Then by
2.19
, we have that

\displaystyle t(\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle=(\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell}))^{c},\text{ and}

\displaystyle t(\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{W}))

\displaystyle=(\rank(\mathcal{W}_{1}),\rank(\mathcal{W}_{2}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{W}_{k}))^{c}

which of course must be equal. This implies that
k=\ell
and that
\rank(\mathcal{V}_{i})=\rank(\mathcal{W}_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq\ell
. Therefore, since all modules are free direct summands of
\mathcal{O}^{d}
, we can then find some
g\in\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
such that
g\overline{\mathcal{V}}=\overline{\mathcal{W}}
. Hence,

\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})=\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{W}})=\mathbf{Stab}(g\overline{\mathcal{V}})=g\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})g^{-1}

showing that
g
belongs to the normalizer of
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})
. However,
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})
is a parabolic subgroup and by
[
SGA3
, Exp. XXII, 5.8.5]
parabolic subgroups are their own normalizer, thus
g\in\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})
. This means that
\overline{\mathcal{W}}=g\overline{\mathcal{V}}=\overline{\mathcal{V}}
, so the stabilizer construction is injective.

Finally, any parabolic is locally and up to conjugation a standard parabolic (
11
) corresponding to some ordered partition
m_{1}+\ldots m_{\ell+1}=d
. Let
\{e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{d}\}
be the standard basis of
\mathcal{O}^{d}
and let
\mathcal{V}_{i}=\Span(e_{1},\ldots,e_{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{i}})
. From the description occurring in the proof of
2.18
, one sees that such a standard parabolic is the stabilizer of the flag

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}.

Then, applying the inverse conjugation to this flag will produce a flag whose stabilizer is locally the initial parabolic. Thus, the map
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
is also surjective as a map of sheaves. Therefore it is an isomorphism as claimed, finishing the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.22
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. The map

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}}).

is an isomorphism of sheaves.

Proof.

Locally, this map becomes the map
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}}
of
2.21
which is an isomorphism. Thus, it is also an isomorphism globally.
∎

2.D.
Type Morphisms for the Inner Case

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d=n+1
. We want to define a type morphism for lowered flags which encodes the sequence of ranks of submodules occurring in a given flag as an increasing tuple. However, the submodules participating in a flag need not be of constant rank, and so this type morphism must instead land in the locally constant sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{n}
of (
4
). Nevertheless, in the case that a flag is comprised of constant rank modules it is clear what we desire the type morphism to be and therefore it is straightforward to define the type morphism for the presheaf
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
of (
12
). Namely, we consider the map of presheaves

(13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{P}_{n}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell}))

with the trivial flag
0\subseteq\mathcal{E}
being mapped to the empty tuple
()
. Since
\mathcal{V}_{\ell}
is constant rank and not equal to
\mathcal{E}
by assumption,
\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell})\leq n
and so this map indeed lands in
\mathcal{P}_{n}
. Then, since
\mathcal{P}_{n}
is itself a sheaf, this map of presheaves will induce a unique map from the sheafification of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
into
\mathcal{P}_{n}
, which by
2.6
is
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Definition 2.23
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d=n+1
. The
type morphism
for the sheaf of lowered flags
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
is the sheaf morphism

t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}\colon\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}

induced from the presheaf morphism (
13
) above. For a section
\overline{r}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}(S)
we define the sheaf of
flags of type
\overline{r}
in
\mathcal{E}
to be the fiber of
t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}
over
\overline{r}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{E}}=t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}^{-1}(\overline{r})\subseteq\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. It is similarly straightforward to define a type morphism from
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}
to
\mathcal{P}_{n}
. However, since the rank of any constant rank right ideal in
\mathcal{A}
is a multiple of
d
, we define the map of presheaves

(14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{P}_{n}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{1})}{d},\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{2})}{d},\ldots,\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{\ell})}{d}\right).

Definition 2.24
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. The
type morphism
for the generalized Severi-Brauer sheaf
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
is the sheaf morphism

t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}\colon\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}

induced from the presheaf morphism (
14
) above. For a section
\overline{r}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}(S)
we define the
Severi-Brauer sheaf of type
\overline{r}
to be the fiber of
t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}
over
\overline{r}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\overline{r},\mathcal{A}}=t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}^{-1}(\overline{r})\subseteq\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}.

Lemma 2.25
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
and set
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}=\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}
. The commutative diagram of
2.21
extends to include type morphisms in the following way.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-0.86108pt\ell\hskip-0.75348pt\textit{a\hskip-0.5382ptg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}

In particular, since the map
(\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt}^{c})
is order
2
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-0.86108pt\ell\hskip-0.75348pt\textit{a\hskip-0.5382ptg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}

commutes.

Proof.

Given a constant rank submodule
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
, we know that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{V})
and that

\rank(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}})=d\cdot\rank(\mathcal{V}).

Thus, we have a commutative diagram of presheaves

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
(
13
)
(
14
)

where the horizontal map sends

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{\ell}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E})).

Thus, the induced diagram of maps of sheaves also commutes, showing that the isomorphism
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
respects the type morphisms and the second diagram commutes as claimed.

Next, the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})

fits into the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
(
13
)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}

which commutes by
2.19
. Thus, the analogues diagram of sheaves with
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
in place of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{E}}
also commutes. Finally, considering that the triangle in the first diagram consists of isomorphisms and combining the above two facts shows the overall commutativity of the first diagram as well, finishing the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.26
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}

Proof.

This diagram locally becomes of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}

which commutes by
2.25
, thus is also commutes globally as claimed.
∎

3.
Hermitian Flags and Outer Severi-Brauer Sheaves

We now consider a group
\mathbf{G}
which is an arbitrary form of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, possibly an outer form, and we define outer analogues of flags and Severi-Brauer sheaves which will once again be isomorphic to the sheaf of parabolic subgroups
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
. Since
\mathbf{G}
is a form of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, we know that
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
for some Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
of constant degree
d
.

3.A.
Hermitian Flags

In the case that
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H})
for a
\mathcal{O}|_{L}
–module
\mathcal{H}
of constant rank
d
, then
\tau
will be the adjoint involution of some hermitian regular form
h\colon f_{*}(\mathcal{H})\times f_{*}(\mathcal{H})\to\mathcal{L}
. We use this hermitian form to define orthogonality. For an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule
\mathcal{V}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
, we set
\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
to be the
\mathcal{L}
–submodule which behaves on
T\in\Sch_{S}
as

T\mapsto\{x\in f_{*}(\mathcal{H})(T)\mid h(x|_{T^{\prime}},v)=0\text{ for all }T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T},v\in\mathcal{V}(T^{\prime})\}.

Example 3.1
.

Recall the split hermitian form of (
7
) which is

\displaystyle h_{d}\colon(\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})\times(\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{O}_{S}\times\mathcal{O}_{S}

\displaystyle((x,y),(z,w))

\displaystyle\mapsto(y^{t}z,w^{t}x).

For an
\mathcal{O}
–submodule
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{O}^{d}
, we set
\mathcal{V}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
to be the usual perpendicular submodule, i.e., the subsheaf of sections
x\in\mathcal{O}^{d}
such that
x^{t}v=0
for all
v\in\mathcal{V}
. If
\mathcal{V}
is of constant rank
r
, then
\mathcal{V}^{\perp}
is of constant rank
d-r
. Furthermore,
(\mathcal{V}^{\perp})^{\perp}=\mathcal{V}
.

Any
(\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O})
–submodule of
\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}
is of the form
\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}
for
\mathcal{O}
–submodules
\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
. Its perpendicular with respect to
h_{d}
is simply

(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2})^{\perp_{h_{d}}}=\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}.

\blacksquare

Ultimately, given a regular hermitian form
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
, we want to define a sheaf of flags within
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
such that when we consider the case of the split hermitian form of (
7
) our new sheaf is isomorphic with
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
. In this way, these new sheaves will be outer twists of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
. The idea will be to look at flags in
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
which are symmetric with respect to orthogonality, i.e., those such that

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))=(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})),

meaning that
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp_{h}}=\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-i}
since the perpendicular operation is order reversing on submodules. However, there is a pitfall which we must avoid. Keeping
Example

3.1
in mind, symmetric flags of
\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}
–submodules in the split hermitian form will appear as

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell-1}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}

and we would like to simply map this to the flag
0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
to achieve an isomorphism with
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
. Unfortunately, it may occur that
\mathcal{V}_{1}=\mathcal{V}_{2}\neq\mathcal{O}^{d}
, and thus this is not a valid section of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
, even if the starting flag is valid, meaning we have
\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\neq\mathcal{V}_{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell-1}^{\perp}
. For example, consider a flag

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{4}

of constant rank submodules with
\rank(\mathcal{V}_{i})=i
. Then, the flag

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{3}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{4}\times\mathcal{O}^{4}

has
\mathcal{O}
–ranks
(2,3,5,6)
and is a valid section of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
which is symmetric with respect to the perpendicular operation. However, its naive projection onto the first factor produces
(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{4})
which is not a valid lowered flag. To forbid such flags from occuring, we introduce the notion of the
\mathcal{L}
–gap of an inclusion of submodules. It will be a locally constant integer value, so we define it via sheafification.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
L\to S
be a finite étale cover of schemes of degree
k
with corresponding commutative
\mathcal{O}
–algebra
\mathcal{L}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Rings
. Let
\mathcal{M}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Ab
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{L}
–module of constant rank
d
. We define the sheaf of length
2
flags of
\mathcal{L}
–submodules of
\mathcal{M}
which are locally direct summands,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}^{2}\colon\Sch_{S}

\displaystyle\to\Sets

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\left\{\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W}\mid\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{M}|_{T}\text{ are }\mathcal{L}|_{T}\text{--submodules, and}\\
\text{they are locally direct summands}\end{array}\right\}.

Note that here we allow sections where
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{W}
. Over any
T\in\Sch_{S}
where
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
, which happens locally, we will have that
\mathcal{M}|_{T}\cong\mathcal{M}_{1,T}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{M}_{k,T}
for finite locally free
\mathcal{O}|_{T}
–modules
\mathcal{M}_{i,T}
all of constant rank
d
. Based on this, we define a presheaf

\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}^{2}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets

of length
2
flags consisting of split constant rank
\mathcal{L}
–submodules which are direct summands. It behaves on
T\in\Sch_{S}
simply as

T\mapsto\O

if
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\not\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
, and it behaves as

T\mapsto\left\{\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{V}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{W}_{k}\mid\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{W}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i,T}\text{ are direct summands,}\\
\text{and they have constant }\mathcal{O}|_{T}\text{--rank}\end{array}\right\}

if
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
. The restriction maps come from submodule restriction within
\mathcal{M}
and therefore may to involve permutations of the factors of
\mathcal{O}^{k}
depending on the local isomorphisms
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
chosen.

Lemma 3.3
.

The canonical injection
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}^{2}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
has the universal property of sheafification and thus shows that

(\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}^{2})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}.

Proof.

Any
\mathcal{L}
–submodule
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{M}
locally takes the form

\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\ldots\mathcal{V}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{1,T}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{M}_{k,T}=\mathcal{M}|_{T}

over any
T\in\Sch_{S}
where
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
since this is the form of any
(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
–submodule. If the submodule
\mathcal{V}
is locally a direct summand then each
\mathcal{V}_{i}
must be a locally direct summand of
\mathcal{M}_{i,T}
. Because
\mathcal{M}
was assumed to be finite locally free as an
\mathcal{L}
–module, each
\mathcal{M}_{i,T}
is finite locally free as an
\mathcal{O}
–module and this
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is also finite locally free as an
\mathcal{O}
–module. Thus, localizing further if necessary, we may assume that each
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is of constant
\mathcal{O}|_{T}
–rank. Thus, any section
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{W}\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}(S)
locally belongs to the image of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
. The same hold for any section of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
, which shows that we have the universal property of sheafification as desired.
∎

Definition 3.4
.

Let
L\to S
be an étale cover of schemes of degree
k
with corresponding commutative
\mathcal{O}
–algebra
\mathcal{L}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Rings
. Let
\mathcal{M}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Ab
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{L}
–module of constant rank
d
. We define a map of presheaves

\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}\to\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}

by requiring that for each
T\in\Sch_{S}
for which
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
, and so
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}(T)
is nonempty, it acts as

\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{V}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{W}_{k}\mapsto\min(\{\rank|_{\mathcal{O}|_{T}}(\mathcal{W}_{i})-\rank_{\mathcal{O}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{i})\mid 1\leq i\leq k\})

and of course it is simply the inclusion
\O\hookrightarrow\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}
when
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}(T)=\O
.

Since
\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}
is a sheaf, the above map induces a unique map of sheaves

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}\colon\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}\to\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}

which we call the
\mathcal{L}
–gap.

As a first comment, we point out that the presheaf map defined above is actually well defined (despite possible permutations appearing in the restriction maps of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{2}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
) since the image is invariant under permutations of the factors. Intuitively, the
\mathcal{L}
–gap aims to capture the maximal
n
such that we have a commutative diagram of injections of
\mathcal{L}
–modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{L}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists}

Stated naively like this we do not get a notion which is stable under base change, so the
\mathcal{L}
–gap locally captures this idea and then combines the result into a locally constant section. In the problematic example given above,

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{3}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{4}\times\mathcal{O}^{4}

it is clear that

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{3}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp})=0

and so we will avoid such flags by disallowing subsequent components from having an
\mathcal{L}
–gap of zero.

Using the
\mathcal{L}
–gap, we define the sheaf of
\mathcal{L}
–lowered
flags.

Definition 3.5
.

We define the presheaf
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
to be the subpresheaf of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
of those flags of constant
\mathcal{O}
–rank submodules which are locally direct sumamnds

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})

where each
\mathcal{V}_{i}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
is also an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule and where
\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i+1})>0
, i.e., sufficiently locally whenever the
\mathcal{L}
–gap is constant it must be non-zero.

We say that a flag

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}

is
\mathcal{L}
–lowered if it consists of
\mathcal{L}
–submodules of
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
which are locally direct summands and if for every
T\in\Sch_{S}
we have

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T})=0\Rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}.

We define the sheaf
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
to be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
consisting of
\mathcal{L}
–lowered flags. Symmetrically, we also have the sheaf
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
of
\mathcal{L}
–raised flags.

Lemma 3.6
.

The sheaf
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
defined above is a sheaf. Furthermore, the canonical inclusion
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
satisfies the universal property of sheafification, showing that

(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}.

Similarly, the inclusion
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
shows that
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{L}
itself is a sheaf, the property of being an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule is stable under base change and gluing. Being
\mathcal{L}
–lowered is stable under base change by definition and therefore we only need to verify that being
\mathcal{L}
–lowered respects gluing.

Without loss of generality, we may work with a section
(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}(S)
over
S
consisting of
\mathcal{L}
–submodules. Assume we have a cover
\{U_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
over which each of the restrictions

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U_{i}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{k_{i}}|_{U_{i}}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{U_{i}})

is
\mathcal{L}|_{U_{i}}
–lowered. Let
T\in\Sch_{S}
and assume that for some index
j
we have

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{j+1}|_{T})=0.

Restricting our cover to
T
produces a cover
\{T_{i}=T\times_{S}U_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
, and for each
i\in I
we have

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T_{i}}}(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{j+1}|_{T_{i}})=0.

Hence, either by truncation within
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
if
j\geq k_{i}
or by the assumption that the restriction to
U_{i}
is
\mathcal{L}|_{U_{i}}
–lowered, we know that

\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T_{i}}=f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{T_{i}}=\mathcal{V}_{j+1}|_{T_{i}}.

Since this holds over the cover of
T
, we conclude that
\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}=f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{j+1}|_{T}
which shows that our original flag is
\mathcal{L}
–lowered. Thus, we have shown that
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
is a sheaf.

Any flag in
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
of course also belongs to
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
, and therefore locally lies in the image of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
by
2.6
. The property that the components are
\mathcal{L}
–submodules which are locally direct summands is preserved by restriction, and so the local sections in
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
will also have this property. Let

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})

be a section of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
which is the restriction of a section from
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
, in particular assume that it is
\mathcal{L}
–lowered. Since the
\mathcal{O}
–ranks of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
are constant and we have that
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i})<\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i+1})
, we know that for all
T\in\Sch_{S}
we have

\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\neq\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}.

Hence, by contrapositive,
\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T})\neq 0
which globally means that

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T})>0.

Therefore, we have shown that sections of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
locally belong to
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
, and so we are done.
∎

The sheaf of hermitian flags we are working towards will be the fixed points in
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
under an order two automorphism related to the perpendicular operation. The perpendicular operation is order reversing on submodule inclusion, therefore if we are given a flag

\overline{\mathcal{V}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell-1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

we can apply the perpendicular operation on the flag as a whole and produce a second flag

\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp_{h}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell-1}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})).

We verify now that the resulting flag remains in
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
with an easy lemma.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
(\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form of constant rank
d
and let
\mathcal{V}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
be an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule which is locally a direct summand and which is finite locally free as an
\mathcal{O}
–module. Then,

(i)

\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}}
is also locally a direct summand,

(ii)

\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}})=2d-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V})
as a locally constant function, and

(iii)

(\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}})^{\perp_{h}}=\mathcal{V}
.

Additionally, if
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W}
are two such
\mathcal{L}
–submodules, then

(iv)

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W})=\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{W}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}})
.

Proof.

(i)
: Since
h\colon f_{*}(\mathcal{H})\times f_{*}(\mathcal{H})\to\mathcal{L}
is regular, it induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}
–modules
