\displaystyle\tilde{h}\colon f_{*}(\mathcal{H})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto h(v,\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})

which is
i^{*}
–semilinear as a map of
\mathcal{L}
–modules. Now, we assume we are working sufficiently locally such that
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})=\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W}
for another
\mathcal{L}
–submodule
\mathcal{W}
. In turn, this means

\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{L})=\tilde{h}(\mathcal{V})\oplus\tilde{h}(\mathcal{W})

where both
\tilde{h}(\mathcal{V})
and
\tilde{h}(\mathcal{W})
are still
\mathcal{L}
–submodules. Then, since
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
is a finite locally free
\mathcal{L}
–modules of rank
d
, we have the canonical double dual isomorphism

f_{*}(\mathcal{H})\cong\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{L}),\mathcal{L})

sending
v\mapsto\textrm{ev}_{v}
. Thus also

f_{*}(\mathcal{H})\cong\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(\tilde{h}(\mathcal{V}),\mathcal{L})\oplus\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(\tilde{h}(\mathcal{W}),\mathcal{L})

and tracing the various isomorphisms, one sees that
\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}}=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(\tilde{h}(\mathcal{W}),\mathcal{L})
and so it is a direct summand of
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
.

(ii)
: Since
\mathcal{V}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
is an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule, after sufficient localization we can assume we are in the setting of
Example

3.1
where we have a submodule of the form

\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}.

Additionally, since we started with a module which is finite locally free as a
\mathcal{O}
–module, the modules
\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
can be assumed to be finite locally free of constant rank. Then,
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2})=\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1})+\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2})
and

\displaystyle\rank_{\mathcal{O}}\big((\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2})^{\perp_{h_{d}}}\big)

\displaystyle=\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp})

\displaystyle=\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp})+\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp})

\displaystyle=d-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2})+d-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1})

\displaystyle=2d-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}).

Thus, globally we have
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}})=2d-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V})
as claimed.

(iii)
: From the definition one sees that
\mathcal{V}\subseteq(\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}})^{\perp_{h}}
, and then we must have equality since they are both finite locally free modules of the same rank by applying
(ii)
twice. Alternatively, one could once again work sufficiently locally to be in the setting of
Example

3.1
where it is obvious to calculate that

\big((\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2})^{\perp}\big)^{\perp}=(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp})^{\perp}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}.

(iv)
: We again work sufficiently locally where
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}
and
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{2}
for constant rank
\mathcal{O}
–modules
\mathcal{V}_{i}
and
\mathcal{W}_{i}
. Then, abbreviating
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i})=r(\mathcal{V}_{i})
, we simply compute that

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(\{d-r(\mathcal{V}_{2})-(d-r(\mathcal{W}_{2})),d-r(\mathcal{V}_{1})-(d-r(\mathcal{W}_{1}))\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(\{r(\mathcal{W}_{2})-r(\mathcal{V}_{2}),r(\mathcal{W}_{1})-r(\mathcal{V}_{1})\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{2}).

Hence, since the equality holds locally we also have

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W})=\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{W}^{\perp_{h}}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}})

globally as claimed.
∎

3.7
shows that we obtain an order two automorphism

(15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp_{h}}.

However, since the perpendicular operation is order reversing it sends lowered flags to raised flags and vice versa

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form. There are mutually inverse sheaf isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp_{h}}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{\perp_{h}}

which fit into the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
(
15
)
(
15
)

Proof.

It is immediate from
3.7
(iii)
that if these maps are well defined, then they are mutually inverse sheaf isomorphisms and fit into the proposed commutative diagrams. So, consider an
\mathcal{L}
–lowered flag
0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
in
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
. If for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
we have

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\perp_{h}}|_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp_{h}}|_{T})=0,

for some index
i
, then this means by
3.7
(iv)
that
\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T})=0
, so since we started with an
\mathcal{L}
–lowered flag we have
\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{T}=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}
. Then applying the perpendicular operator again, we see

\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\perp_{h}}|_{T}=0=\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp_{h}}|_{T}

so the resulting flag is
\mathcal{L}
–raised. Symmetrically, the perpendicular of a raised flag is a lowered flag. Thus, the proof is finished.
∎

This shows that naively applying the perpendicular operation does not quite give us what we want. Instead, we use the universal property of the sheafification of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
to define an order two isomorphism of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
.

Lemma 3.9
.

There is a unique order two automorphism
\pi_{h}\colon\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
which restricts to (
15
) on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
. It is equivalently defined by the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{h}}
(
15
)

or as the composition

\pi_{h}\colon\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}

where the first isomorphism is from
3.8
and
\varphi
is uniquely induced by both
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
being sheafifications of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
(it is analogous to
\varphi_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}
as in
2.8
).

Proof.

This is immediate since
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}=(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}})^{\sharp}
by
3.6
.
∎

Example 3.10
.

By combining the description of
\varphi_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}
appearing in
2.8
with the perpendicular operation, we can give an example of how the map
\pi_{h}
behaves. Say we have a section of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}(S)
which restricts to flags of constant length over a decomposition
S=X\sqcup Y\sqcup W
. Writing the flag vertically as in
2.8
,
\pi_{h}
will appear as

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{X}&f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{Y}&f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{W})\\
(f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{X}&f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{\ell}|_{W})\\
&\vdots&\\
(f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{X}&\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W})\\
&\vdots&\\
(\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{X}&\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W})\\
&\vdots&\\
(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{X}&\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W})\\
(0&0&0)\end{array}\mapsto\begin{array}[]{ccc}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{X}&f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{Y}&f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{W})\\
(f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{X}&f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp_{h}}|_{W})\\
&\vdots&\\
(f_{*}(\mathcal{H})|_{X}&\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp_{h}}|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-j_{2}}^{\perp_{h}}|_{W})\\
&\vdots&\\
(\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp_{h}}|_{X}&\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}+1-j_{1}}^{\perp_{h}}|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-j_{1}}^{\perp_{h}}|_{W})\\
&\vdots&\\
(\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}^{\perp_{h}}|_{X}&\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}^{\perp_{h}}|_{Y}&\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp_{h}}|_{W})\\
(0&0&0),\end{array}

simply applying the perpendicular operation to the non-zero proper submodule portion within each column.

\blacksquare

The sheaf we actually want to work with is the subsheaf of fixed points of
\pi_{h}
.

Definition 3.11
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form of constant rank
d
. The presheaf of
constant rank hermitian flags
of
\mathcal{H}
is
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
which sends
T\in\Sch_{S}
to

\left\{(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}(T)\mid\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp_{h}}=\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right\}.

The sheaf of
hermitian lowered flags
of
\mathcal{H}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\colon\Sch_{S}

\displaystyle\to\Sets

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\{\overline{\mathcal{V}}\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}(T)\mid\pi_{h}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})=\overline{\mathcal{V}}\}.

Lemma 3.12
.

The canonical inclusion
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
satisfies the universal property of sheafification, showing that

(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)})^{\sharp}=\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
3.6
since
\pi_{h}
restricts to the perpendicular operation on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
by definition in
3.9
.
∎

Example 3.13
.

Here we show that our
\mathcal{L}
–gap based definitions have solved the problems discussed after
Example

3.1
. Recall from
Example

3.1
that for the split hermitian form
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h_{d})=(f\colon S\sqcup S\to S,(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})
of (
7
) we have that

(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2})^{\perp_{h_{d}}}=\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}

for
\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
submodules. Therefore, for
T\in\Sch_{S}
, a section of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h_{d})}(T)
is of the form

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell-1}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell-1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}|_{T}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}|_{T}

where each
\mathcal{V}_{j}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-j}^{\perp}
is of constant
\mathcal{O}|_{T}
–rank and the
\mathcal{L}|_{T}
–gaps between subsequent components are strictly larger than zero. The submodules
\mathcal{V}_{j}
themselves may not be of constant
\mathcal{O}|_{T}
–rank, but we may restrict further until they are. Then, it is immediate that the assumption on
\mathcal{L}
–gaps implies that
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i})<\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i+1})
for all
i
, and so projecting onto the first factor produces a section

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}|_{T})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}(T).

However, some localization was required to make the ranks of the first factors constant.

In the converse direction, it is clear that if we start with a section
(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}|_{T})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}(T)
then

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h_{d})}(T)

is a valid symmetric flag with strictly positive
\mathcal{L}|_{T}
–gaps. Hence we have an injective map of presheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})

which is not presheaf surjective, but is locally surjective. It therefore induces an isomorphism of the respective sheafifications

(16)

\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}.

In fact, this isomorphism can be written a bit more explicitly. For a section in
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
, denote

\varphi_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d})=(0\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d})

to be its image in
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{+}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
. Then, the isomorphism (
16
) acts as

(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d})\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}).

However, checking that this is indeed how the isomorphism appears is a bit cumbersome, so we omit those details.

\blacksquare

The action of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
on
\mathcal{H}_{d}
induces an action on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}
in which
a\in\mathbf{GL}_{d}
acts by

(17)

\displaystyle a\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0\subseteq a\mathcal{V}_{1}\times(a^{-1})^{t}\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq a\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times(a^{-1})^{t}\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})

and
\overline{1}\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
acts by

\displaystyle\overline{1}\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}).

By
3.12
, this extends uniquely to an action on
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}

Lemma 3.14
.

Consider a regular hermitian form
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
of constant rank
d
and denote its corresponding
(\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
–torsor by
\mathcal{K}
. Then,

\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\cong\mathcal{K}\wedge^{\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}.

Proof.

The hermitian forms
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
and
(S\sqcup S\to S,(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})
are locally isomorphic and any isomorphism between them extends to an isomorphism between
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
and
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}
. Thus,
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
will be the twist of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}
by the
\mathbf{Aut}(S\sqcup S\to S,(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})=\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–torsor corresponding to
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
, i.e., exactly

\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\cong\mathcal{K}\wedge^{\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}

as claimed.
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form of constant rank
d
. The natural action of
\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
on
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
induces an action on
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
. For
B\in\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
, it behaves as

B\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))=(0\subseteq B\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq B\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})).

Proof.

We argue that the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
on
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
induces an action on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
, which will then induce the action claimed above via sheafification. To see that we obtain a well-defined action on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
, we need to check that the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
preserves the properties of a flag being symmetric and having strictly positive
\mathcal{L}
–gaps.

For symmetry,
\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
is defined by the property that for
B\in\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
we have
h(Bx,By)=h(x,y)
, and therefore

(B\mathcal{V})^{\perp_{h}}=B(\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}}).

Thus, for a section
(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
we will have that

(B\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp_{h}}=B(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\perp_{h}})=B\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-j},

and so the resulting flag is also symmetric.

Regarding
\mathcal{L}
–gaps, for any two subsequent components in a section of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
, they are locally of the form

\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}

and likewise a section
B\in\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
locally assumes the form
(B_{1},(B_{1}^{-1})^{t})
for some
B_{1}\in\mathbf{GL}_{d}
. Then, applying
B_{1}
preserves the
\mathcal{O}
–ranks of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{1}
while applying
(B_{1}^{-1})^{t}
preserves the ranks of
\mathcal{V}_{2}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. Therefore, it is clear that

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}((B_{1},(B_{1}^{-1})^{t})(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2})\subseteq(B_{1},(B_{1}^{-1})^{t})(\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{2}).

Since the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
locally fixes the
\mathcal{L}
–gap, it fixes it globally as well. It therefore also preserves the property of
\mathcal{L}
–gap being strictly positive, and so we are done.
∎

3.B.
Outer Severi-Brauer Sheaves

We now return to considering an Azumaya algebra
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
of constant degree
d
with involution of the second kind. Similarly to how we considered
\mathcal{L}=f_{*}(\mathcal{O}|_{L})
–submodules which are of constant
\mathcal{O}
–rank in the previous section, here will we be concerned with
\mathcal{L}
–ideals of
f_{*}(\mathcal{B})
which are locally direct summands and which are finite locally free as
\mathcal{O}
–modules. Given a right ideal
\mathcal{I}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{B})
, its left annihilator

\displaystyle{{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}\colon\Sch_{S}

\displaystyle\to\Ab

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\{b\in f_{*}(\mathcal{B})(T)\mid b\mathcal{I}|_{T}=0\}

is a left ideal of
f_{*}(\mathcal{B})
. Using the involution
\tau
we can obtain another right ideal
\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{B}
. This operation is also order reversing and will therefore send lowered flags to raised flags. Thus, we work with presheaves of constant rank objects here as well after a preparatory lemma.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of degree rank
d
. Thus,
f_{*}(\mathcal{B})
is of constant rank
2d^{2}
. Let
\mathcal{I}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{B})
be a right
\mathcal{L}
–ideal which is locally a direct summand and which is finite locally free as a
\mathcal{O}
–module. Then,

(i)

\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I})
is a right
\mathcal{L}
–ideal which is also locally a direct summand,

(ii)

\tau\big({{}^{0}}(\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}))\big)=\mathcal{I}
, i.e., this is an order two operation,

(iii)

\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I})
is also finite locally free as an
\mathcal{O}
–module,

(iv)

\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{I})
as a locally constant function is always divisible by
d
, and

(v)

\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}))=2d^{2}-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{I})
which is then also divisible by
d
.

Additionally, for two such right
\mathcal{L}
–ideals
\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{J}
,

(vi)

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{J})=\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{J})\subseteq\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}))
.

Proof.

Since all the claims are local in nature, we may assume that we are working with
(f\colon S\sqcup S\to S,(\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\tau_{d})
as in (
6
) and that
\mathcal{I}\subseteq f_{*}(\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}))=\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
is of constant
\mathcal{O}
–rank and a direct summand. Therefore, it is of the form

\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}_{1}\times\mathcal{I}_{2}

where
\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
are two
\mathcal{O}
–right ideals of constant rank which are direct summands of
\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
.

(i)
: By
2.16
, each
\mathcal{I}_{i}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{i})
for direct summands
\mathcal{V}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
. Equivalently,

\mathcal{I}_{i}=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}c_{1}&\cdots&c_{d}\end{bmatrix}\mid c_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{i}\right\}

writing the
c_{j}
as columns. Thus, it is clear that

{{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{i}=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}r_{1}\\
\vdots\\
r_{d}\end{bmatrix}\mid r_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp}\right\}

writing the
r_{j}
as rows. Thus,
({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{i})^{t}=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp})
which is a direct summand of
\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
since
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
is a direct summand. Overall, we have that

\tau_{d}({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I})=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp})\times\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp})

which is an
\mathcal{L}
–ideal and a direct summand of
\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
.

(ii)
: This follows from
(i)
since
(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp})^{\perp}=\mathcal{V}_{i}
.

(iii)
: This is clear from
(i)
since each
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp}
are finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–modules.

(iv)
: We know that
\rank(\mathcal{I}_{i})=r_{i}d
for some integers
0\leq r_{i}\leq d
, and therefore

\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{I})=(r_{1}+r_{2})d

which is divisible by
d
.

(v)
: Once again using the description in
(i)
, we know that
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\perp})=d-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i})
and therefore

\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{i})^{t})=d^{2}-r_{i}d.

Together, this means that

\displaystyle\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}))

\displaystyle=d^{2}-r_{1}d+d^{2}-r_{2}d

\displaystyle=2d^{2}-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{I})

as claimed.

(vi)
: We may work with
\mathcal{I}_{1}\times\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{1}\times\mathcal{J}_{2}
and set
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{J}_{i})=s_{i}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\tau({{}^{0}}(\mathcal{J}_{1}\times\mathcal{J}_{2}))\subseteq\tau({{}^{0}}(\mathcal{I}_{1}\times\mathcal{I}_{2})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(({{}^{0}}\mathcal{J}_{2})^{t}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{J}_{1})^{t}\subseteq({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{2})^{t}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(\{d^{2}-r_{2}-(d^{2}-s_{2}),d^{2}-r_{1}-(d^{2}-s_{1})\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(\{s_{2}-r_{2},s_{1}-r_{1}\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{I}_{1}\times\mathcal{I}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{1}\times\mathcal{J}_{2})

as claimed.
∎

Definition 3.17
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant degree
d
. We define

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets

to be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{H}}
where the flags additionally consist of right
\mathcal{L}
–ideals. They will still be locally direct summands with constant
\mathcal{O}
–rank and with strictly positive
\mathcal{L}
–gap between subsequent components of the flags.

By
3.16
, we have an order two automorphism of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
given by

(18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{B}))

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{B})).

The next step is to define a sheaf which is the sheafification of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
.

Definition 3.18
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant degree
d
. We define the sheaf

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets

to be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{B})}
where each component of the flag is a right
\mathcal{L}
–ideal and the flag is
\mathcal{L}
–lowered. In particular, each component will be locally a direct summand and which is finite locally free as an
\mathcal{O}
–module.

Symmetrically, we define
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
to be the sheaf of
\mathcal{L}
–raised flags of right
\mathcal{L}
–ideals in
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{B})}
.

Lemma 3.19
.

The canonical inclusion
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
has the universal property of sheafification and so
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
. Thus, the order two automorphism (
18
) induces an order two automorphism

\pi_{\mathcal{B}}\colon\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}.

Proof.

It is sufficient to show the claim about sheafification and thus it suffices to see that any section of
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
locally belongs to the image of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
. However, the sections of
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
which consist of right
\mathcal{L}
–ideals will belong to
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
, and therefore locally belong to
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
. Then, because they consist of right ideals they will locally belong to
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
, so we are done.
∎

This map
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}
has a similar description as
\pi_{h}
in
Example

3.10
except with ideals
\mathcal{I}
in place of submodules
\mathcal{V}
and
\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I})
in place of
\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}}
.

Definition 3.20
.

Let
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant degree
d
. We define

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets

to be the subpresheaf of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
consisting of flags fixed by the order two automorphism of (
18
). Similarly, we define

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets

to be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B}}
consisting of flags fixed by the automorphism
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}
of
3.19
. This is the
generalized Severi-Brauer sheaf
of
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
.

Lemma 3.21
.

The sheaf
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
defined above is in fact a sheaf. Furthermore, the inclusion
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
has the universal property of sheafification, thus showing that

(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}.

Proof.

The is the analogous statement to
3.12
and follows from a similar argument.
∎

Example 3.22
.

If we look at the split Azumaya algebra with with involution of the second kind as in (
6
), for
T\in\Sch_{S}
the sections of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\mathcal{L},(\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}))}(T)
are
\mathcal{L}|_{T}
–lowered flags of the form

0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\times\mathcal{J}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{J}_{\ell}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}

where each
\mathcal{I}_{j}
and
\mathcal{J}_{j}
are right ideals of constant rank in
\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}
. We also know from the calculation in
3.16
(i)
that

\tau_{d}({{}^{0}}(\mathcal{I}_{j}\times\mathcal{J}_{j}))=({{}^{0}}\mathcal{J}_{j})^{t}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{j})^{t}.

Thus, the sections of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}(T)
appear as

0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}.

Since these flags have strictly positive
\mathcal{L}
–gaps, the map of presheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}))

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\subseteq(\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T})^{2})

is injective and locally surjective (though not presheaf surjective). Thus, it induces an isomorphism of the sheafifications

(19)

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}.

Similar to the description given for (
16
), denoting

\varphi_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}))=(0\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{\ell}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}))\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})},

the map (
19
) appears as

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}))

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{J}_{\ell})^{t}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{J}_{1})^{t}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})).

There is a natural action of
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}
where
\varphi\in\mathbf{PGL}_{d}
acts by

\displaystyle\varphi\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\varphi\mathcal{I}_{1}\times\theta^{\prime}(\varphi)({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\varphi\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times\theta^{\prime}(\varphi)({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}),

using the outer automorphism
\theta^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}
defined in
Section

1.B
, and
\overline{1}\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
acts by

\displaystyle\overline{1}\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0\subseteq({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\times\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\times\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})|_{T}).

This action extends to a
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–action on
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 3.23
.

Let
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant degree
d
and let
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
be the corresponding
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–torsor. Then,

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}\cong\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\wedge^{\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}.

Proof.

This is the analogous fact to
3.14
and follows by a similar argument.
∎

Lemma 3.24
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant degree
d
. The natural action of
\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
on
f_{*}(\mathcal{B})
by conjugation extends to an action on
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},t)}
. This extended action is also by conjugation, or equivalently by left multiplication since the flags are comprised of right ideals. That is, for
b\in\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
it acts by

b\cdot(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{B}))=(0\subseteq b\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq b\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{B})).

Proof.

We show that this action restricts to a well-defined action on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
which in turn means that it is well-defined on
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
through sheafification. Thus, we argue that the action on a section of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
preserves the properties of being a symmetric flag of ideals and of having strictly positive
\mathcal{L}
–gaps. However,
\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
is defined by the property that for
b\in\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
we have
b=\tau(b^{-1})
. Therefore, if
(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{B}))\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
is a section, then

\tau({{}^{0}}(b\mathcal{I}_{j}))=\tau({{}^{0}}(\mathcal{I}_{j})b^{-1})=\tau(b^{-1})\cdot\tau({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{j})=b\mathcal{I}_{\ell+1-j}

and so the resulting section is also symmetric. Regarding
\mathcal{L}
–gaps, the action of
\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
sufficiently locally is by module isomorphism on each factor of
\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
and so the ranks of submodules are preserved, which implies that the action of
\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
preserves
\mathcal{L}
–gaps. Hence, the action is well-defined on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
and we are done.
∎

When the Azumaya algebra is the endomorphism algebra of a regular hermitian form
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
of constant rank
d
, the sheaves
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
and
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}
will coincide as expected by sending each subspace occurring in a flag to a suitable right ideal. In particular, let
\mathcal{V}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
be an
\mathcal{L}
–subspace and define the right
\mathcal{L}
–ideal

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{V})\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\cong f_{*}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H})),

where the isomorphism
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\cong f_{*}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H}))
is due to
[
St
, Tag 01SB]
.

Lemma 3.25
.

With notation as above,

(i)

if
\mathcal{V}
is locally a direct summand of
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
then
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}
is locally a direct summand of
f_{*}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H}))
,

(ii)

if
\mathcal{V}
is finite locally free as an
\mathcal{O}
–module then so is
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}
, and

(iii)

\tau_{h}({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}})=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},(\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h}})}
.

Additionally, if
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W}
are two such
\mathcal{L}
–submodules, then

(iv)

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{W}})=d\cdot\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W})
. In particular, if
\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W})
is strictly positive then so is
\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{W}})
.

Proof.

Working locally, we may assume that
\mathcal{V}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
is a direct summand which is of constant
\mathcal{O}
–rank and show that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}
has similar properties.

(i)
: If
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})=\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W}
for another
\mathcal{L}
–submodule
\mathcal{W}
, then

\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{V})\oplus\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{W})

and we see that
\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{V})
is a direct summand as required.

(ii)
: Localizing further, we may assume that we are working with the split regular hermitian form of (
7
). Then
\mathcal{H}_{d}=(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d})
and
\mathcal{V}
takes the form

\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}

for constant rank
\mathcal{O}
–submodules
\mathcal{V}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
. Then, since
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}
consists of
\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}
–linear maps, we have that

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{1})\times\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\times\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}

which is locally free of constant rank
d\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1})+d\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2})
.

(iii)
: It also suffices to show that this holds for the split hermitian form. For
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}
we have that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\times\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}
and the calculations in
3.16
(i)
show that

\displaystyle\tau_{d}({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp})\times\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d},(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2})^{\perp_{h_{d}}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},(\mathcal{V}^{\perp_{h_{d}}})}

as desired.

(iv)
: Localizing as in
(ii)
our
\mathcal{L}
–submodules appear as
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{2}=\mathcal{W}
and our ideals appear as

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\times\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{W}_{1}}\times\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{W}}.

Using the calculations above we compute

\displaystyle\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{W}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(\{d\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{W}_{1})-d\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}),d\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{W}_{2})-d\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2})\})

\displaystyle=d\cdot\min(\{\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{W}_{1})-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{W}_{2})-\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2})\})

\displaystyle=d\cdot\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W})

and therefore
\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{W}})=d\cdot\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W})
holds globally as well, as claimed.
∎

Proposition 3.26
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form of constant rank
d
and consider the associated Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})
. Then, the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{H})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}_{1}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}_{\ell}}\subseteq\mathcal{H}),

is well defined, i.e. it sends
\mathcal{L}
–lowered symmetric flags to
\mathcal{L}
–lowered symmetric flags of ideals, and it is an isomorphism of sheaves. Additionally, this map is equivariant under the actions of
\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\cong\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}
given in
3.15
and
3.24
.

Proof.

To see that the map is well-defined, we instead argue that the analogous map on presheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}

\displaystyle(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{H})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}_{1}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}_{\ell}}\subseteq\mathcal{H}),

is a well-defined presheaf morphism. If this is the case, it will induce the claimed map by sheafification. The fact that the map between presheaves is well-defined follows directly from
3.25
and the fact that symmetric flags are send to symmetric flags follows because the condition that
\mathcal{V}_{i}=\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-i}^{\perp_{h}}
implies that

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}_{i}}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},(\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-i}^{\perp_{h}})}=\tau_{h}({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-i}}).

Now, to see that the map is an isomorphism, we may show that it is an isomorphism locally when the hermitian form becomes the split hermitian form. In that case, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
(
16
)
(
19
)

where the vertical maps are isomorphisms and the bottom map is the isomorphism of
2.17
. Thus, our map in question, the top map, is an isomorphism as well.

To see that the map is equivariant as claimed, it is immediate to check that for
b\in\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
and
\mathcal{V}\subset f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
an appropriate submodule, we have

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},(b\mathcal{V})}=\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),b\mathcal{V})=b\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{L}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{V})=b\cdot\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{V}}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

3.C.
Isomorphisms with Parabolic Subgroups

Mirroring the steps of
Section

2.C
, we first consider the case when
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\cong\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}
for a regular hermitian form
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
. Given a section

\overline{\mathcal{V}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H}))\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)},

we set
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})\subseteq\mathbf{G}
to be the stabilizer of this section under the action of
3.15
. Likewise, given a section

\overline{\mathcal{I}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}|_{L}}(\mathcal{H})))\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})},

we set
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}})\subseteq\mathbf{G}
to be the stabilizer under the action of
3.24
.

Lemma 3.27
.

The stabilizers
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{V}})
and
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}})
are parabolic subgroups of
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\cong\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}
.

Proof.

We may show that the subgroup is parabolic locally to conclude that it is a parabolic subgroup. Sufficiently locally, we are in the case of
Example

3.13
and
\mathbf{GL}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}\cong\mathbf{GL}_{d}
where a section
a\in\mathbf{GL}_{d}
acts as in (
17
) on flags of submodules. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d})

which we know is a parabolic subgroup by
2.18
. The claim for
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}})
then follows from
3.26
.
∎

Lemma 3.28
.

Let
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form of constant rank
d
and set
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}=\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}
. The two maps
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
and
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
which send a flag to its stabilizer are both isomorphisms. Furthermore, they fit into the commutative diagram of isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}}

where the horizontal map is the isomorphism of
3.26
.

Proof.

Working sufficiently locally, this becomes exactly the situation of
2.21
. Thus, it follows globally as well.
∎

In general, for an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
which is not an endomorphism algebra and a section
\overline{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
we also set
\mathbf{Stab}(\overline{\mathcal{I}})\subseteq\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
to be the stabilizer under the action of
3.24
.

Corollary 3.29
.

Let
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant degree
d
. Then, the map

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}},

which sends a flag to its stabilizer, is an isomorphism of sheaves.

Proof.

Locally, this becomes an isomorphism of the form
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}\to\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}}
as in
3.28
and thus it is also an isomorphism globally.
∎

3.D.
Type Morphisms for the Outer Case

Here we define type morphisms for hermitian flags and outer Severi-Brauer sheaves which are compatible with the type morphism (
10
) of
[
SGA3
]
and thus land in
\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))
for an appropriate group
\mathbf{G}
. We obtain these maps by twisting the maps defined in
Section

2.D
.

We start with the split hermitian form of constant rank
d=n+1
of (
7
) and consider the composition

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq_ConFlag_type}}\mathcal{P}_{n}

of the isomorphism from
Example

3.13
with the type map defined in the inner case. Since
\mathcal{P}_{n}
is a sheaf, this induces a type morphism

(20)

\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}.

Recall that
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
acts on
\mathcal{P}_{n}
via the isomorphism
\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt}^{\vee}\colon\mathcal{P}_{n}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}
, and thus by acting trivially with
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
we obtain a
\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–action on
\mathcal{P}_{n}
.

Lemma 3.30
.

In the setting above, the map

\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}

is
\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–equivariant.

Proof.

It is sufficient to show that the composition
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}
is equivariant. We know that both the isomorphism
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}
and the map (
13
) are equivariant under the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, so we only need to check
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–equivariance. Consider a section

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}

of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.93747pt\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}
. Under the map in consideration, this is sent to the tuple

(\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell})).

However, after applying the action of
\overline{1}\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
we have the section

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}

which is sent to the tuple

\displaystyle(\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\perp}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp}),\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(d-\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell}),\ldots,d-\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}),d-\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\rank(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\rank(\mathcal{V}_{2}),\ldots,\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell}))^{\vee}.

Thus, we also have
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–equivariance as desired.
∎

Definition 3.31
.

let
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form of constant rank
d=n+1
and let
\mathcal{K}
be its associated
\mathbf{GL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–torsor. Set
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
. Due to equivariance, we may twist the map (
20
) by the torsor
\mathcal{K}
. Using
3.14
and (
8
), we obtain a map

\widetilde{t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.20552pt\ell\hskip-1.05487pt\textit{a\hskip-0.75346ptg}}}\colon\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\to\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))

which we call the
type morphism
for the sheaf
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
.

We now work with the split Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant rank
d=n+1
as in (
6
) and
Example

3.22
. We have a composition

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq_ConIdeal_type}}\mathcal{P}_{n}

where the first map is the isomorphism of
Example

3.22
. This induces a type map

(21)

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}.

The sheaf
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}
has the
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–action described in
Example

3.22
, and by acting trivially with
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}
we also have a
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–action on
\mathcal{P}_{n}
.

Lemma 3.32
.

In the setting above, the map

\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}

is
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–equivariant.

Proof.

It is clear that it is equivariant for the
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}
–action. To check equivariance for the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–action, we check that the underlying map

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{d\hskip-1.07639pte\hskip-1.07639pta\hskip-1.07639ptl}_{((\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})),\tau_{d})}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}

is equivariant. A section

0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\times({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})

is mapped to the tuple

\left(\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{1})}{d},\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{2})}{d},\ldots,\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{\ell})}{d}\right).

After applying
\overline{1}\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
, we obtain the section

0\subseteq({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t}\times\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t}\times\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})

which is mapped to the tuple

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\rank(({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{\ell})^{t})}{d},\ldots,\frac{\rank(({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{2})^{t})}{d},\frac{\rank(({{}^{0}}\mathcal{I}_{1})^{t})}{d}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d^{2}-\rank(\mathcal{I}_{\ell})}{d},\ldots,\frac{d^{2}-\rank(\mathcal{I}_{2})}{d},\frac{d^{2}-\rank(\mathcal{I}_{1})}{d}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(d-\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{\ell})}{d},\ldots,d-\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{2})}{d},d-\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{1})}{d}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{1})}{d},\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{2})}{d},\ldots,\frac{\rank(\mathcal{I}_{\ell})}{d}\right)^{\vee}.

This shows
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–equivariance as claimed.
∎

Definition 3.33
.

Let
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second type of constant degree
d
and let
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
be its associated
\mathbf{PGL}_{d}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
–torsor. Set
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
. Due to equivariance, we may twist the map (
21
) by the torsor
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
. Using
3.23
and (
8
), we obtain a map

\widetilde{t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}\colon\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}\to\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))

which we call the
type morphism
for the sheaf
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
.

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular hermitian form of constant rank
d=n+1
. Set
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}=\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}
. The commutative diagram of
3.28
extends to include type morphisms in the following way.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-0.86108pt\ell\hskip-0.75348pt\textit{a\hskip-0.5382ptg}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}

In particular, since the map
(\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt}^{c})
is order
2
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}(\mathcal{H}),\tau_{h})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{t_{\mathcal{F}\hskip-0.86108pt\ell\hskip-0.75348pt\textit{a\hskip-0.5382ptg}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}}

commutes.

Proof.

Locally this becomes the statement of
2.25
, and so the claims hold globally as well.
∎

Corollary 3.35
.

Let
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
be an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind of constant degree
d
and let
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{U}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
. We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\hskip-1.29167pt\textit{f}(\mathcal{D}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptn}(\mathbf{G}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{t_{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\hskip 6.02777pt}^{c}}

Proof.

This holds globally since it holds locally by
3.34
.
∎

4.
Idempotents and Levi Subgroups

For
\mathbf{G}
a linear algebraic group, we denote by
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{G}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
the sheaf of pairs of parabolic and Levi subgroups. In detail, for
T\in\Sch_{S}
, we have

\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{G}}(T)=\{(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})\mid\mathbf{P}\subset\mathbf{G}|_{T}\text{ is a parabolic, }\mathbf{L}\subset\mathbf{P}\text{ is a Levi subgroup}\}.

Of course, there is an obvious surjective map

(22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{G}}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{P}.

In
Section

3
we showed how
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
can be realized as
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{(\mathcal{B},\tau)}
for an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second type
(L\to S,\mathcal{B},\tau)
, or as
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
if the algebra is the endomorphism algebra of a regular hermitian form
(L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
. We now develop analogous descriptions of
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{G}}
in terms of Azumaya algebras and flags.

4.A.
Inner Case

We begin with the inner case when
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
or
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
as in
Section

2
.

4.A.i.
Direct Summands and Idempotents

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d=n+1
. We wish to consider a sheaf of direct sum decompositions of
\mathcal{E}
. Naively, these are tuples of submodules
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
such that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}
. From such a tuple we can construct a flag of partial direct sums

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}

and we wish to use this construction to define a map into
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{GL}_{\mathcal{E}}}\cong\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
. However, we therefore need to impose suitable conditions on our tuples of submodules in order to ensure that the resulting flags will be lowered flags. This motivates the following definition.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
. Let
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
be a tuple of submodules of
\mathcal{E}
such that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}
. We call such a tuple
lowered
if for all
T\in\Sch_{S}
and all
1\leq i\leq\ell
,

\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=0\Rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}=0\text{ for all }j\geq i.

We define a functor
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
, called the
Stiefel functor of
\mathcal{E}
, which behaves on
T\in\Sch_{S}
by

T\mapsto\{(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\mid\ell\geq 0,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\neq 0,\mathcal{E}|_{T}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{j},\text{and the tuple is lowered}\}

with the restriction along a map
T^{\prime}\to T
in
\Sch_{S}
being defined by

(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\mapsto(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{T^{\prime}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{T^{\prime}})

where
k\in\{1,\ldots,\ell+1\}
is the largest integer such that
\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{T^{\prime}}\neq 0
. I.e., since
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
is lowered, it naively restricts to a length
\ell+1
tuple of the form
(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{T^{\prime}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{T^{\prime}},0,\ldots,0)
and we truncate the zeroes.

The definition of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
using lowered objects and restriction involving truncation is reminiscent of the definition of the sheaf
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
in
2.4
, and we show below that indeed
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
is a sheaf as well. It similarly can be viewed as the sheafification of a presheaf of direct sum decompositions involving summands of constant rank.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
. We define the presheaf
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
which behaves as

T\mapsto\{(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\mid\ell\geq 0,\mathcal{V}_{i}\neq 0,\mathcal{E}|_{T}=\oplus_{i=1}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{i}\text{ and each }\mathcal{V}_{i}\text{ is constant rank}\}.

Lemma 4.3
.

The functor
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
defined above is a sheaf. Furthermore, the inclusion

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

satisfies the universal property of sheafification, showing that
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

If we know that any section
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}(T)
must have
\ell<d
, then we may mirror the proof of
2.5
. However,
\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\neq 0
by assumption and therefore when we find a cover
\{U_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
over which all
\mathcal{V}_{j}
are of constant rank, there must exist
i_{0}\in\mathcal{I}
with
\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}|_{U_{i_{0}}}\neq 0
. Thus,
\rank(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i_{0}}})\geq 1
for all
1\leq j\leq\ell+1
, so

d=\rank(\mathcal{E}|_{U_{i_{0}}})=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell+1}\rank(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i_{0}}})\geq\ell+1,

meaning that
\ell<d
. Therefore, we may follow the arguments of
2.5
and see that
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
is a sheaf.

Once we know that
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
is a sheaf, it is clear that because each component in a tuple is locally of constant rank that therefore any section of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
locally belongs to
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}
, which demonstrates that
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
and we are done.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

Symmetrically, a tuple
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
with
\mathcal{E}=\oplus_{i=1}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{i}
is
raised
if for all
T\in\Sch_{S}
,

\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{T}=0\Rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}=0\text{ for all }j\leq i

and these form a sheaf
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
whose restriction maps involve truncation on the left. We also have that the inclusion map
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}
realizes
