(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}
and therefore we have a uniquely defined isomorphism of sheaves

(23)

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}\colon\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}

which is constant on sections from
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Similar to the raising isomorphism described in
2.8
, the isomorphism
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
has a “sliding up” effect. For example, for a lowered tuple

(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}(S)

of length
\ell+1
, there will be a disjoint decomposition
X=W_{j_{1}}\sqcup W_{j_{2}}\sqcup\ldots\sqcup W_{j_{m}}\sqcup W_{\ell+1}
such that the restriction of the tuple to
W_{j}
is of constant length
j
. Thus, writing the tuple vertically, it will appear as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}

with zeroes above the staircase pattern. This flag is mapped by
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
to the raised flag

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+2-{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{2}+1-j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}+1-j_{1}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+2-j_{1}}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+2-{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{j_{m}+1-j_{2}}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+2-j_{2}}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+2-{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{j_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\ell+2-j_{m}}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{W_{\ell+1}})}

which now has zeroes below the staircase pattern.

\blacksquare

We will make use of the isomorphism
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
from the preceding remark in the next
Section

4.B
where we will need the following small lemma.

Lemma 4.5
.

The isomorphism
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}\colon\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}
of (
23
) preserves the length of a tuple. That is, given a section
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}(T)
for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
, it has the property that
\mathcal{V}_{j}\neq 0
by definition for all
1\leq j\leq\ell+1
. Then, the section

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})=(\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{\ell+1})

is also of length
\ell+1
and
\mathcal{W}_{j}\neq 0
for all
1\leq j\leq\ell+1
.

Proof.

We consider a cover
\{U_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
over which the lowered tuple
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
restricts to tuples in
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Since
\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\neq 0
globally and the tuple is lowered, there exists at least one index
i_{0}\in I
for which

(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U_{i_{0}}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}|_{U_{i_{0}}})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i_{0}})

is a length
\ell+1
tuple of non-zero constant rank direct summands. Furthermore, for all
i\in I
, the restriction has length
\leq\ell+1
. Now, let

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})=(\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{m})

for some integer
m
. Since
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
acts as the identity on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{E}}
, we also have that
(\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{m})|_{U_{i}}
has length
\leq\ell+1
for all
i\in I
while

(\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{W}_{m})|_{U_{i_{0}}}=(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U_{i_{0}}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}|_{U_{i_{0}}})

has length exactly
\ell+1
. Thus, if
m>\ell+1
this would imply that
\mathcal{W}_{m}=0
which in turn implies that
\mathcal{W}_{1}=0
since this tuple is raised, however this contradicts the fact that
\mathcal{W}_{1}|_{U_{i_{0}}}=\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U_{i_{0}}}\neq 0
. Thus,
m\leq\ell+1
and since
\mathcal{W}_{\ell+1}\neq 0
because
\mathcal{W}_{\ell+1}|_{U_{i_{0}}}=\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}|_{U_{i_{0}}}\neq 0
we conclude that
m=\ell+1
. Lastly, we then likewise have for all
1\leq j\leq\ell+1
that

\mathcal{W}_{j}|_{U_{i_{0}}}=\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i_{0}}}\neq 0

showing that
\mathcal{W}_{j}\neq 0
for all
j
. Thus, we are done.
∎

Now, as was our goal when defining lowered tuples, the construction which takes a lowered tuple of direct summands to a flag produces our desired map of sheaves.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
. We have a well-defined map of sheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}).

In particular, a lowered tuple produces a lowered flag and this construction is compatible with the restriction maps within the sheaves. Furthermore, this is a surjective maps of sheaves.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
be a lowered tuple and consider the associated flag

0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}.

Let
T\in\Sch_{S}
and assume that for some
1\leq i\leq\ell

(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{i})|_{T}=(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{i}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{i+1})|_{T}.

In particular, this means that
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}\subseteq(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{i})|_{T}
. However, the direct sum decomposition
\mathcal{E}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{j}
restricts as well, so we have
\mathcal{E}|_{T}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}
. Therefore, the above inclusion forces
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|_{T}=0
, and then since the tuple is lowered,
\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}=0
for all
j\geq i+1
as well. This means that

\mathcal{E}|_{T}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}

and so

(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{i})|_{T}=\mathcal{E}|_{T}=(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{i}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{i+1})|_{T}.

Thus, the resulting flag is lowered as desired.

It is clear from construction that this map is compatible with the restriction maps of the two sheaves.

To see that this is a surjective map of sheaves, consider a flag

0\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E}

in
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
. By assumption, the
\mathcal{W}_{j}
are locally direct summands of
\mathcal{E}
, and since surjectivity is a local property we may assume that they are already direct summands. Thus, we may find
\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}
such that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{W}_{\ell}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}
, and then we may find
\mathcal{V}_{\ell}
such that
\mathcal{W}_{\ell}=\mathcal{W}_{\ell-1}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell}
, and so on ending with
\mathcal{V}_{1}=\mathcal{W}_{1}
. This produces a tuple
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
with the property that

\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j}=\mathcal{W}_{j}

for all
j
. We claim that this is a lowered tuple. Indeed, if for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
and
j
we have
\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}=0
, this means that
\mathcal{W}_{j-1}|_{T}=\mathcal{W}_{j}|_{T}
which implies that
\mathcal{W}_{j-1}|_{T}=\mathcal{E}|_{T}=\mathcal{W}_{j}|_{T}
since the flag is lowered. This also implies that
\mathcal{W}_{k}|_{T}=\mathcal{E}|_{T}
for all
k\geq j
, and therefore
\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{T}=0
for all such
k
. This shows that the tuple is lowered and so
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
is a section which maps to
(0\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{E})\in\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Thus, we have a surjective map of sheaves.
∎

Equivalently, a section in
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
can be viewed as an ordered tuple of pairwise orthogonal idempotents in
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})
, namely the projections onto each of the direct summands. This allows us to generalize to non-neutral Azumaya algebras.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. A tuple
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})
of sections of
\mathcal{A}
such that
e_{i}^{2}=e_{i}
for all
i
,
e_{i}e_{j}=0
for
i\neq j
, and
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}e_{i}=1
is called a
tuple of pairwise orthogonal idempotents
. Such a tuple is additionally called
lowered
if for all
T\in\Sch_{S}
and all
1\leq i\leq\ell
,

e_{i}|_{T}=0\Rightarrow e_{j}|_{T}=0\text{ for all }j\geq i.

We define the functor
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
to send
T\in\Sch_{S}
to

\left\{(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\mid\begin{array}[]{c}\ell\geq 0,\;e_{i}\in\mathcal{A}(T),e_{\ell+1}\neq 0,\text{this is a lowered}\\
\text{tuple of pairwise orthogonal idempotents}\end{array}\right\}.

Restriction along a morphism
T^{\prime}\to T
in
\Sch_{S}
is defined by

(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\mapsto(e_{1}|_{T^{\prime}},e_{2}|_{T^{\prime}},\ldots,e_{k}|_{T^{\prime}})

where
k\in\{1,\ldots,\ell+1\}
is the largest integer such that
e_{k}|_{T^{\prime}}\neq 0
. I.e., since
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})
is lowered, it naively restricts to a length
\ell+1
tuple of the form
(e_{1}|_{T^{\prime}},\ldots,e_{k}|_{T^{\prime}},0,\ldots,0)
and we truncate the zeroes.

Lemma 4.8
.

The functor
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
is a sheaf.

Proof.

Ultimately, we mirror the proof of
2.5
here as well. In order to do so, we check that any tuple of pairwise orthogonal idempotents
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})
has
\ell<d
. However, this follows because such a tuple defines a direct sum decomposition

\mathcal{A}=e_{1}\mathcal{A}\oplus\ldots e_{\ell+1}\mathcal{A}

by right ideals which each have rank divisible by
d
. Therefore, for a cover over which the ideals
e_{i}\mathcal{A}
become constant rank there will be at least one scheme in the cover over which
\rank(e_{i}\mathcal{A})\geq d
for all
i
, and therefore

d^{2}=\rank(\mathcal{A})=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}\rank(e_{i}\mathcal{A})\geq d(\ell+1)

and therefore
\ell<d
. Thus, as was done in
4.3
we may adapt the proof of
2.5
and see that
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a sheaf.
∎

In the case when
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})
is an endomorphism algebra, we can relate
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}
in the following way (which we eventually show produces an isomorphism). For an idempotent
e\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})
, we set
\mathcal{I}_{e}=e\cdot\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})
, which is a right ideal which is also a direct summand (since
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})=e\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})\oplus(1-e)\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})
).

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}
–module of constant rank
d
. There is an isomorphism of sheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\Img(e_{1}),\ldots,\Img(e_{\ell+1}))

\displaystyle(\pi_{\mathcal{V}_{1}},\ldots,\pi_{\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}})

\displaystyle\;\reflectbox{$\mapsto$}\;(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})

where
\pi_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}
is the projection onto
\mathcal{V}_{i}
sending other
\mathcal{V}_{j}
to zero. Furthermore, this isomorphism fits into a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the downward map on the right is the surjection of
4.6
, the bottom map is the isomorphism of
2.17
, and so the downward map on the left takes the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}

\displaystyle(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{e_{1}}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\ell})}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})).

Proof.

It is immediate that the composition

(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\mapsto(\pi_{\mathcal{V}_{1}},\ldots,\pi_{\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}})\mapsto(\Img(\pi_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}),\ldots,\Img(\pi_{\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}}))

is the identity.

In the converse direction, let
\pi_{i}
be the projection onto
\Img(e_{i})
. Since
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})
is a pairwise orthogonal tuple of idempotents, this means that
\pi_{i}\circ e_{j}=0
if
i\neq j
. Additionally,
\pi_{i}\circ e_{i}=e_{i}
, and therefore

\pi_{i}=\pi_{i}\circ\Id_{\mathcal{E}}=\pi_{i}(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\ell+1})=\pi_{i}\circ e_{i}=e_{i}.

Thus the composition

(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\mapsto(\Img(e_{1}),\ldots,\Img(e_{\ell+1}))\mapsto(\pi_{\Img(e_{1})},\ldots,\pi_{\Img(e_{\ell+1})})

is also the identity. Hence, we have an isomorphism as claimed.

To see that the downward map on the left has the claimed form, we follow a tuple
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}
through the diagram. First, going right, it gets sent to the section

(\Img(e_{1}),\ldots,\Img(e_{\ell+1}))\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}.

Then, because the
e_{i}
are pairwise orthogonal,
\Img(e_{i})\oplus\Img(e_{j})=\Img(e_{i}+e_{j})
and so going down we arrive at the flag

0\subseteq\Img(e_{1})\subseteq\Img(e_{1}+e_{2})\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\ell})\subseteq\mathcal{E}.

Finally, going left we obtain a flag of right ideals whose
i^{\text{th}}
component is

\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E},\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})).

We argue that this is
\mathcal{I}_{(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})}
. For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})
, the composition
(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})\circ\varphi
will have image contained in
\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})
, and therefore

\mathcal{I}_{(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})}\subseteq\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E},\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})).

Conversely, since
e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i}
is itself an idempotent, any section in
\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})
is fixed by
e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i}
. Thus, if
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E},\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i}))
then

(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})\circ\varphi=\varphi,

showing that
\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E},\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i}))\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})}
. Thus,

\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E},\Img(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i}))=\mathcal{I}_{(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})}

and the downward map on the left of the diagram has the claimed form.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. We have a surjective map of sheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq e_{1}\mathcal{A}\subseteq(e_{1}+e_{2})\mathcal{A}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\ell})\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}).

Proof.

Given a lowered tuple of pairwise orthogonal idempotents in
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
, they induce a direct sum decomposition of
\mathcal{A}
corresponding to the section

(e_{1}\mathcal{A},\ldots,e_{\ell+1}\mathcal{A})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}

and it is clear this defines a map of sheaves
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
. We may then compose this with the map of
4.6
to obtain a map
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
which sends
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})
to

0\subseteq e_{1}\mathcal{A}\subseteq e_{1}\mathcal{A}\oplus e_{2}\mathcal{A}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq e_{1}\mathcal{A}\oplus\ldots\oplus e_{\ell}\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}.

Each component of this flag is a right ideal, so this lands in
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Finally, since the idempotents are pairwise orthogonal we have that

e_{1}\mathcal{A}\oplus\ldots\oplus e_{i}\mathcal{A}=(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{i})\mathcal{A}

for all
i
. Therefore, our proposed map of sheaves is well-defined.

Locally, this becomes the map
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}
of
4.9
which is surjective, so the map is surjective globally as well.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

There is of course also a sheaf of raised tuples of idempotents,
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and a unique raising isomorphism

\rho_{\mathcal{I}}\colon\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}

which appears similarly to
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
as described in
4.4
, just with idempotents in place of submodules. It also preserves the length of a tuple. In the case of an endomorphism algebra, we have a commutative diagram of isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathcal{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathcal{S}}}

where the downward map on the left is the isomorphism of
4.9
and the downward map on the right is its analogue.

\blacksquare

4.A.ii.
Parabolics and Levis

Consider
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
for an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d=n+1
. Given a section
\overline{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(S)
, we will associate it to a parabolic and Levi pair by first using it to define a cocharacter of
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
and then using the limit subgroup construction of
Section

1.E.iii
. As an initial remark, we point out that the construction in this section for the inner case works equally well using the straightforward cocharacter

\displaystyle\lambda_{\overline{e}}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{-i}e_{i}.

However, a more complicated construction will be required in
Section

4.B.iii
and so for compatibility we will use that construction here as well.

Recall, for example from
[
SGA3
, Expose XXII, 1.11]
and the fact that
\mathbb{G}_{m}=D_{S}(\mathbb{Z})
in their notation, that as a sheaf on
\Sch_{S}
, there character group of
\mathbb{G}_{m}

\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\textrm{Grp}}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{G}_{m})=\underline{\mathbb{Z}}

is the sheaf of locally constant integers. In particular, for a scheme
T\in\Sch_{S}
and a section
\underline{a}\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}}(T)
, we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{T}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{T}

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\mapsto t^{\underline{a}}

for the associated character which is defined by the property that over a cover
\{U_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
where
\underline{a}|_{U}=n_{i}
is constant, it behaves as
t\mapsto t^{n_{i}}
as usual. Thus, for a section
x\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(T)
we have that
x^{\underline{a}}
is the gluing of the local elements
(x|_{U_{i}})^{n_{i}}
. Rephrasing this, these characters have the property that
(x^{\underline{a}})|_{U}=(x|_{U})^{\underline{a}|_{U}}
for all
U\in\Sch_{T}
.

Likewise, given a section
\overline{e}\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(T)
, the length of the tuple is a locally constant positive integer by construction and therefore we have a section
\underline{a}(\overline{e})\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}}(T)
encoding the length of
\overline{e}
. We use this to define the cocharacter

(24)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\overline{e}}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{\underline{a}(\overline{e})+1-2i}e_{i}.

where the terms
1
and
2i
in
\underline{a}(\overline{e})+1-2i
are constant sections of
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
. We define our cocharacters this way in order to have the following lemma necessary later on.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
(or more generally a separable algebra) and consider a section
\overline{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
\rho_{\mathcal{I}}(\overline{e})=(e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{\ell+1}^{\prime})
be the associated raised section. Then, we have an alternative expression for the cocharacter
\lambda_{\overline{e}}
, namely

\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{\underline{a}(\overline{e})+1-2i}e_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{-\underline{a}(\overline{e})-1+2i}e^{\prime}_{\ell+2-i}.

Proof.

Let
\overline{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(T)
. It is sufficient to show the equality holds locally over a scheme
U\in\Sch_{T}
where the length section
\underline{a}(\overline{e})
is constant since we may choose a cover consisting of such schemes. Say
\underline{a}(\overline{e})|_{U}=k
. In particular,

(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})|_{U}=(e_{1}|_{U},\ldots,e_{k}|_{U})=(e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{\ell+1}^{\prime})|_{U}

which means that
(e_{\ell+2-i}^{\prime})|_{U}=e_{k+1-i}|_{U}
since the raised tuple truncates on the left under restrictions. Now, we may simply compare the expressions

\displaystyle\left.\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{\underline{a}(\overline{e})+1-2i}e_{i}\right)\right|_{U}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t|_{U}^{k-1}e_{1}|_{U}+t|_{U}^{k-3}e_{2}|_{U}+\ldots+t|_{U}^{-k+1}e_{k}|_{U}+t|_{U}^{-k-1}0+\ldots+t|_{U}^{k+1-2(\ell+1)}0

and

\displaystyle\left.\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{-\underline{a}(\overline{e})-1+2i}e^{\prime}_{\ell+2-i}\right)\right|_{U}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t|_{U}^{-k+1}e_{k}|_{U}+t|_{U}^{-k+3}e_{k-1}|_{U}+\ldots+t|_{U}^{k-1}e_{1}|_{U}+t|_{U}^{k+1}0+\ldots+t|_{U}^{-k-1+2(\ell+1)}0

and see that they are equal.
∎

We now assign a parabolic and Levi pair
(\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}),\mathbf{L}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}))
to
\overline{e}
as reviewed in
Section

1.E.iii
.

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
for an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d=n+1
. There is an isomorphism of sheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{G}}

\displaystyle\overline{e}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}),\mathbf{L}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}))

Proof.

It suffices to show that the map is an isomorphism when
\mathcal{A}=\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
and so
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{n}
since the general map with be locally of this form, and a map of sheaves which is locally an isomorphism is an isomorphism globally.

Assuming that
\mathcal{A}=\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})
, it is then sufficient to show that the standard parabolic and Levi pairs, i.e., those where the parabolic subgroup contains the Borel subgroup of invertible upper triangular matrices and the Levi subgroup consists of block diagonal matrices, have a unique preimage in
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\Mat_{d}(\mathcal{O})}
, which we will denote by
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{d}
. This is sufficient since any other pair
(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})
is conjugate to a pair of the above form. Say that
(g\mathbf{P}g^{-1},g\mathbf{L}g^{-1})
is a standard pair and so there is a unique
\overline{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})
such that

(\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}),\mathbf{L}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}))=(g\mathbf{P}g^{-1},g\mathbf{L}g^{-1}).

By
1.11
, this means that

(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})=(\mathbf{P}(g^{-1}\lambda_{\overline{e}}g),\mathbf{L}(g^{-1}\lambda_{\overline{e}}g)).

It is straightforward using the definition of the cocharacter to see that

g^{-1}\lambda_{\overline{e}}g=\lambda_{g^{-1}\overline{e}g}

where
g^{-1}\overline{e}g=(g^{-1}e_{1}g,\ldots,g^{-1}e_{\ell+1}g)\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{d}
. Of course, if there was another
\overline{e^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}_{d}
also producing
(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})
, then by using
1.11
again we would have that
g\overline{e^{\prime}}g^{-1}
produces the same standard pair as
\overline{e}
, and so by uniqueness
g\overline{e^{\prime}}g^{-1}=\overline{e}
, meaning that
g^{-1}\overline{e}g
is the unique section which produces
(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})
.

Furthermore, it is sufficient to assume that
(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})
is a standard pair of constant type, i.e., that
\mathbf{P}
has constant type under the type morphism of (
10
), since we may otherwise localize until it does have constant type. Thus, we may assume that

\mathbf{L}\cong\mathbf{GL}_{n_{1}}\times\mathbf{GL}_{n_{2}}\times\ldots\mathbf{GL}_{n_{p}}

sitting inside
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
as

\mathbf{L}=\left\{\left[\begin{array}[]{c:c:c:c}B_{1}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{2}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{p}\end{array}\right]\mid B_{i}\in\mathbf{GL}_{n_{i}}\right\}

and then
\mathbf{P}
sits inside
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
as

\mathbf{P}=\left\{\left[\begin{array}[]{c:c:c:c}B_{1}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&*\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&*\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&*\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{2}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&*\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&*\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&*\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{p}\end{array}\right]\mid B_{i}\in\mathbf{GL}_{n_{i}}\right\}

Let
e_{i}
be idempotents corresponding to the block decomposition of
\mathbf{L}
, i.e., let

e_{1}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c:c:c:c}I_{n_{1}}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\end{array}\right],e_{2}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c:c:c:c}0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&I_{n_{2}}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\end{array}\right],\ldots,e_{p}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c:c:c:c}0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\\
\hline\cr 0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&0\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&I_{n_{p}}\end{array}\right].

Let
\overline{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{p})
. This tuple of idempotents is lowered since
e_{i}|_{T}\neq 0
for any
T\in\Sch_{S}
, thus
\overline{e}\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{d}(S)
. Additionally, since
\overline{e}
is constant length, the exponents of
t
appearing in
\lambda(\overline{e})
are all constant sections. A quick calculation then shows that
\mathbf{L}=\mathbf{L}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})
. However, this is not the unique cocharacter whose centralizer is
\mathbf{L}
. Any cocharacter
\lambda\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{GL}_{d}
whose centralizer is
\mathbf{L}
must map into the center of
\mathbf{L}
, which is isomorphic to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{p}
. Thus, it is of the form

\lambda(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{p}t^{\underline{a_{i}}}e_{i}

for powers
\underline{a_{i}}\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
. In order to have
\mathbf{Cent}_{\mathbf{GL}_{d}}(\lambda)=\mathbf{L}
and not a larger subgroup, all
\underline{a_{i}}
must be locally distinct, and in fact any such cocharacter will have centralizer
\mathbf{L}
. However, not all of these cocharacters come from a section in
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{d}
, only those with
\{\underline{a_{1}},\ldots,\underline{a_{p}}\}=\{p-1,p-3,\ldots,-p+3,-p+1\}
and these come from the sections in
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{d}(S)
which are permutations of the ordered tuple
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{p})
. Thus, we must argue that from among these cocharacters, only
\lambda_{\overline{e}}
has
\mathbf{P}
as its limit parabolic subgroup.

Consider such a cocharacter
\lambda(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{p}t^{a_{i}}e_{i}
with
\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}\}=\{p-1,p-3,\ldots,-p+3,-p+1\}
. Write a matrix
B\in\mathbf{GL}_{d}
as a
p\times p
block matrix

B=\left[\begin{array}[]{c:c:c:c}B_{11}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{12}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ldots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{1p}\\
\hline\cr B_{21}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{22}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ldots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{2p}\\
\hline\cr\vdots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\vdots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\vdots\\
\hline\cr B_{p1}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{p2}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ldots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{pp}\end{array}\right]

with square blocks on the diagonal of size according to the block decomposition of
\mathbf{L}
and thus possibly rectangular off-diagonal blocks. Performing a calculation analogous to the one in
Example

1.10
, we see that

\lambda(t)B\lambda(t^{-1})=\left[\begin{array}[]{c:c:c:c}B_{11}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&t^{a_{1}-a_{2}}B_{12}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ldots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&t^{a_{1}-a_{p}}B_{1p}\\
\hline\cr t^{a_{2}-a_{1}}B_{21}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{22}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ldots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&t^{a_{2}-a_{p}}B_{2p}\\
\hline\cr\vdots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\vdots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ddots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\vdots\\
\hline\cr t^{a_{p}-a_{1}}B_{p1}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&t^{a_{p}-a_{2}}B_{p2}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&\ldots\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@add@cssclass&B_{pp}\end{array}\right]=[t^{a_{i}-a_{j}}B_{ij}]_{i,j=1}^{p}.

The resulting limit parabolic subgroup will agree with
\mathbf{P}
if and only if all the powers of
t
appearing in the lower diagonal blocks are negative (and so those appearing in the upper diagonal blocks will be positive). However, this occurs exactly when the tuple
(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{p})
is decreasing, meaning that
a_{i}=p+1-2i
and so

\lambda=\lambda_{\overline{e}}.

Therefore,
\overline{e}\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(S)
is the unique section such that

(\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}),\mathbf{L}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}))=(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L}),

which finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

Section

1.E.iii
describes how any cocharacter
\lambda\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{G}
produces a parabolic-Levi pair
(\mathbf{P}(\lambda),\mathbf{L}(\lambda))
. Conversely,
4.13
says that every parabolic-Levi pair arises uniquely from this construction when one considers cocharacters associated in a particular way to lowered tuples of pairwise orthogonal idempotents. However, in the case when the base scheme
S
is connected, the fact that every pair
(\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L})
arises from at least some cocharacter was already included in
[
Gil
, 7.3.1(1)]
.

\blacksquare

Proposition 4.15
.

Let
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}
for an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebra of constant degree
d
. The isomorphism of
4.13
fits into the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the downward maps are those of
4.10
and (
22
) respectively, and the bottom isomorphism is the map of
2.22
.

Proof.

Let
T\in\Sch_{S}
and consider a section
\overline{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{d\hskip-1.72218pte\hskip-1.50696ptm\hskip-1.50696ptp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(T)
. Let
\mathcal{I}_{j}=(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{j})\cdot\mathcal{A}|_{T}
so that

\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}}=(0\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{A}|_{T})

is the image of
\overline{e}
in
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{B}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}(T)
. Since
\overline{e}
maps to
(\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}),\mathbf{L}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}))
in
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{G}}(T)
, to show that the diagram commutes we need to verify that

\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})=\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}).

Recall from
Section

1.E
that for
T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T}
, the group
\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})(T^{\prime})
is

\left\{h\in\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime})\mid\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t)h|_{T_{m}^{\prime}}\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t^{-1})\in\Img(\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{a})\xrightarrow{\textrm{res}}\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{m}))\right\}.

where
T^{\prime}_{a}\to T^{\prime}
and
T^{\prime}_{m}\to T^{\prime}
are the relatively affine schemes corresponding to the sheaves of commutative
\mathcal{O}|_{T^{\prime}}
–algebras
\mathcal{O}|_{T^{\prime}}[t]
and
\mathcal{O}|_{T^{\prime}}[t,t^{-1}]
, respectively. Thus, as a first step we will identify
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{a})
and
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{m})
. By definition,

\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{a})=\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime}_{a})^{\times},

and so we must identify
\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime}_{a})
. Since the morphism
T^{\prime}_{a}\to T^{\prime}
is affine, for any Zariski cover
\{U_{i}\to T^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
by affine open subschemes, the pulled back cover
\{T^{\prime}_{a}\times_{T^{\prime}}U_{i}\to T^{\prime}_{a}\}_{i\in I}
is also a Zariski cover by affine open subschemes. Then, since
\mathcal{A}
is quasi-coherent, this means that

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime}_{a}\times_{T^{\prime}}U_{i})

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{A}(U_{i})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U_{i})}\mathcal{O}(T^{\prime}_{a}\times_{T^{\prime}}U_{i})

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{A}(U_{i})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U_{i})}\mathcal{O}(U_{i})[t]

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{A}(U_{i})[t]

where the variable
t
commutes with all elements of
\mathcal{A}(U_{i})
. From here, an easy analysis of the sheaf exact sequence (where one can view
\mathcal{A}(U_{i})[t]\cong\oplus_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{A}(U_{i})
as a module) shows that

\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime}_{a})\cong\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime})[t].

Likewise, we obtain that

\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime}_{m})\cong\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime})[t,t^{-1}].

Therefore,
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{a})
and
\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{m})
are simply the invertible elements in the the above two algebras, respectively. Additionally,

\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{-i}e_{i}\in\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{m}).

Now, we will show that
\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})\subseteq\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})
. For a section
h\in\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})(T^{\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime})^{\times}
, we have that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t)h\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t^{-1})

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{-i}e_{i}\right)h\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}t^{i}e_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{\ell+1}t^{-i+j}e_{i}he_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=-\ell}^{\ell}t^{n}\left(\sum_{-i+j=n}e_{i}he_{j}\right)

must only involve non-negative powers of
t
, and thus

\sum_{-i+j=n}e_{i}he_{j}=0

for all
-\ell\leq n\leq-1
. Equivalently, by flipping some negative signs and changing indices,

\sum_{i=n+1}^{\ell+1}e_{i}he_{i-n}=0

for all
1\leq n\leq\ell
. Now, for any pair of indices
p>q
, we may take the above expression with
n=p-q
and multiply on the left and right to obtain

e_{p}he_{q}=e_{p}\left(\sum_{i=p-q+1}^{\ell+1}e_{i}he_{i-p+q}\right)e_{q}=0.

In particular, one sees that for any
1\leq k\leq\ell+1
,

(e_{k+1}+\ldots+e_{\ell+1})h(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{k})=0.

Writing
f_{k}=e_{1}+\ldots+e_{k}
, which is also an idempotent, this is
(1-f_{k})hf_{k}=0
, which can equivalently be written as

hf_{k}=f_{k}hf_{k}.

We now claim that the element
h
stabilizes the right ideal
f_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}
. Indeed, any section of
hf_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}h^{-1}
is of the form

(hf_{k})ah^{-1}=(f_{k}hf_{k})ah^{-1}=f_{k}(hf_{k}ah^{-1})\in f_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}.

So
hf_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}h^{-1}\subseteq f_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}
. The same reasoning applies to
h^{-1}\in\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})(T^{\prime})
yielding the reverse inclusion, and thus

hf_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}h^{-1}=f_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}.

This holds for all
1\leq k\leq\ell+1
, showing that in fact

h\in\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})(T^{\prime})

and so we have that
\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})\subseteq\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})
as claimed.

Finally, we argue that
\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})\subseteq\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})
. Consider a section
a\in\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})(T^{\prime})
. Writing
f_{k}=e_{1}+\ldots e_{k}
as above, we have that

af_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}}a^{-1}=f_{k}\mathcal{A}|_{T^{\prime}},

which in particular implies that
af_{k}a^{-1}=f_{k}b
for some section
b\in\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime})
, or equivalently that
af_{k}=f_{k}ba
. However, this means that

f_{k}af_{k}=f_{k}(f_{k}ba)=f_{k}ba=af_{k}

since
f_{k}
is idempotent. Next, we essentially reverse the calculations from the previous case. For any indices
p>q
We have that
(1-f_{q})af_{q}=0
, and so also
e_{p}(1-f_{q})af_{q}e_{q}=0
which expands simply into
e_{p}ae_{q}=0
. Since we have this for all
p>q
, it is immediate that

\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t)a\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t^{-1})\in\mathcal{A}(T^{\prime})[t],

i.e., it does not involve any negative powers of
t
. Likewise, by the same reasoning the same holds for

\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t)a^{-1}\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t^{-1})

which is an inverse for the expression with
a
, and thus

\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t)a\lambda_{\overline{e}}(t^{-1})\in\mathbf{GL}_{1,\mathcal{A}}(T^{\prime}_{a})

meaning that
a\in\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})(T^{\prime})
. This shows that we also have
\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})\subseteq\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}})
and so

\mathbf{Stab}(\mathcal{I}_{\overline{e}})=\mathbf{P}(\lambda_{\overline{e}}).

Thus, the diagram commutes and the proof is finished.
∎

4.B.
Outer Case

We now consider the general case of
\mathcal{P}\hskip-1.93747pt\textit{a\hskip-0.6458ptr}_{\mathbf{G}}
when
\mathbf{G}\cong\mathbf{U}(\mathcal{B},\tau)
for an Azumaya algebra with involution of the second kind. Here, we will need the notion of the
\mathcal{L}
–subrank
of an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule. This will be a locally constant section, i.e., a section of the sheaf
\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}
as in
Section

1.A
, which we define through sheafification. In our application, it will be closely related to the
\mathcal{L}
–gap of
3.4
.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
L\to S
be a finite étale cover of schemes of degree
k
with corresponding commutative
\mathcal{O}
–algebra
\mathcal{L}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Rings
. Let
\mathcal{M}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Ab
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{L}
–module of constant rank
d
. We define the sheaf of
\mathcal{L}
–submodules which are locally direct summands,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}\colon\Sch_{S}

\displaystyle\to\Sets

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\left\{\mathcal{V}\mid\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{M}|_{T}\text{ is an }\mathcal{L}|_{T}\text{--submodule, and}\\
\text{it is locally a direct summand}\end{array}\right\}.

Over any
T\in\Sch_{S}
where
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
, which happens locally, we will have that
\mathcal{M}|_{T}\cong\mathcal{M}_{1,T}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{M}_{k,T}
for finite locally free
\mathcal{O}|_{T}
–modules
\mathcal{M}_{i,T}
all of constant rank
d
. Based on this, we define a presheaf

\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets

of split constant rank
\mathcal{L}
–submodules which are direct summands which behaves on
T\in\Sch_{S}
as

T\mapsto\begin{cases}\O&\text{if }\mathcal{L}|_{T}\not\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}\\
\left\{\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{V}_{k}\mid\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{V}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i,T}\text{ is a direct summand,}\\
\text{and it has constant }\mathcal{O}|_{T}\text{--rank}\end{array}\right\}&\text{if }\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}\end{cases}

with restriction maps coming from submodule restriction within
\mathcal{M}
(and so they may involve permutations of the factors depending on the isomorphisms
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
chosen).

Lemma 4.17
.

The canonical injection
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
has the universal property of sheafification and thus shows that

(\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}})^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}.

Proof.

As argued in the proof of
3.3
, any
\mathcal{L}
–submodule
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{M}
locally takes the form

\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\ldots\mathcal{V}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{1,T}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{M}_{k,T}=\mathcal{M}|_{T}

over any
T\in\Sch_{S}
where
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
. If
\mathcal{V}
is also locally a direct summand, then the assumptions on
\mathcal{M}
mean that
\mathcal{V}
locally belongs to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
. Thus, any section of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
locally belongs to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}
, which justifies the claim.
∎

Definition 4.18
.

Let
L\to S
be an étale cover of schemes of degree
k
with corresponding commutative
\mathcal{O}
–algebra
\mathcal{L}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Rings
. Let
\mathcal{M}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Ab
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{L}
–module of constant rank
d
. We define a map of presheaves

\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}\to\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}

requiring that for each
T\in\Sch_{S}
for which
\mathcal{L}|_{T}\cong(\mathcal{O}|_{T})^{k}
, and so
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}(T)
is nonempty, it acts as

\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{V}_{k}\mapsto\min(\{\rank_{\mathcal{O}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\ldots,\rank_{\mathcal{O}|_{T}}(\mathcal{V}_{k})\})

and of course it is simply the inclusion
\O\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}(T)
when
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{p}\hskip-0.43057pt\ell\hskip-1.29167pt\mathit{i\hskip-0.86108ptt}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}(T)=\O
.

Since
\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}
is a sheaf, the above map induces a unique map of sheaves

\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}}\colon\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{u\hskip-1.29167ptb}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{o\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}}\to\underline{\{0,\ldots,d\}}

which we call the
\mathcal{L}
–subrank
.

The
\mathcal{L}
–subrank is well-defined since the presheaf map is invariant under permutations of the factors and therefore compatible with restrictions. Intuitively, the
\mathcal{L}
–subrank is the locally constant section which encodes the
\mathcal{L}
–rank of the largest free
\mathcal{L}
–module contained in
\mathcal{V}
. We must use
\mathcal{L}
–subrank, instead of simply
\mathcal{L}
–rank, since we must consider
\mathcal{L}
–submodules of
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
which are locally direct summands that are finite locally free as
\mathcal{O}
–submodules but not necessarily finite locally free as
\mathcal{L}
–modules. This is to include submodules of the form
\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}
where
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{1})\neq\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{2})
.

4.B.i.
Orthogonal Summands of Hermitian Forms

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular Hermitian form of constant rank
d
. We wish to consider certain direct sum decompositions of
f_{*}(\mathcal{H})
whose partial sums will produce a flag belonging to
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
. For this purpose, we define the following.

Definition 4.19
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular Hermitian form of constant rank
d
and set
\mathcal{L}=f_{*}(\mathcal{O}|_{L})
. For
T\in\Sch_{S}
we say that a tuple

(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f*(\mathcal{H})}(T)

is
\mathcal{L}|_{T}
–lowered
if all
\mathcal{V}_{j}
are
\mathcal{L}
–submodules and for all
T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T}
we have that

\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T^{\prime}})=0\Rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{T^{\prime}}=0\text{ for all }k\geq j.

Additionally, recall the length preserving isomorphism

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}\colon\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}

of (
23
). For
T\in\Sch_{S}
we define the subset
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}(T)
to be

\left\{(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}(T)\mid\begin{array}[]{l}\text{the tuple is }\mathcal{L}\text{--lowered, and}\\
\mathcal{V}_{i}=\left(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-i}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j}\right)^{\perp_{h}}\\
\text{where }\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})=(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1})\end{array}\right\}.

Intuitively, for a tuple
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
of constant rank submodules, the condition that
\mathcal{V}_{i}=\left(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-i}\mathcal{V}_{j}\right)^{\perp_{h}}
will ensure that the resulting flag of partial sums has the appropriate symmetry property. However, since the length of a tuple may change upon restriction, this naive condition fails to produce a sheaf and therefore we must use the condition with
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
in order to obtain a sheaf.

Lemma 4.20
.

Setting
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Sets
to send
T\in\Sch_{S}
to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(T)
defined above, produces a subsheaf of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
.

Proof.

First we show that
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
is a subpresheaf of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
by showing that the imposed conditions are stable with respect to restrictions. First, being
\mathcal{L}
–lowered is stable under restrictions by definition. Thus, assume that we have a section

(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(T)

for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
and that we have a map of schemes
U\to T
. The restriction of the tuple within
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
is

(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{U})

for some
1\leq k\leq\ell+1
. Now,
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
is an isomorphism of sheaves and thus also compatible with restrictions, however we must keep in mind that restrictions within
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{+}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
involve truncation on the left. Combining this with the fact that
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
is length preserving by
4.5
, then if

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})=(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1})

then

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{U})=(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+2-k}|_{U},\ldots,\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1}|_{U}).

Alternatively, we can write that
(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U})^{\prime}=(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1-k+j})|_{U}
. Thus, to be a member of the set
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(U)
the restricted section must satisfy the conditions that

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{i}|_{U}

\displaystyle=\left(\bigoplus_{j=1\atop j\neq k+1-i}^{k}(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U})^{\prime}\right)^{\perp_{h}}=\left(\bigoplus_{j=1\atop j\neq k+1-i}^{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1-k+j})|_{U}\right)^{\perp_{h}}

\displaystyle=\left(\bigoplus_{j=\ell+2-k\atop j\neq\ell+2-i}^{\ell+1}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j})|_{U}\right)^{\perp_{h}}=\left.\left(\bigoplus_{j\neq\ell+2-i}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j}\right)^{\perp_{h}}\right|_{U}

which is true since it is the restriction of the global condition. Thus, we have that
(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{U})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(U)
and so
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
is a subpresheaf of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}
.

Now, to see that
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
is a sheaf it only remains to show that it satisfies gluing, i.e., that for
T\in\Sch_{S}
, a section of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}(T)
which locally belongs to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
also belongs to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(T)
. Consider such a section

(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{f_{*}(\mathcal{H})}(T)

and a cover
\{U_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
over which the section belongs to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
. First, this means that for all
\mathcal{V}_{j}
, the restrictions
\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i}}
are
\mathcal{L}|_{U_{i}}
–modules. Therefore, each
\mathcal{V}_{j}
is itself an
\mathcal{L}
–module. It remains to show that the tuple is
\mathcal{L}|_{T}
–lowered. Let
T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T}
and assume that for some index
j
we have that
\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T^{\prime}})=0
. By setting
U_{i}^{\prime}=T^{\prime}\times_{T}U_{i}
we obtain the cover
\{U_{i}^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
of
T^{\prime}
. Then, for each
i
we have
\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}|_{U_{i}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i}^{\prime}})=0
which then implies that

\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{U_{i}^{\prime}}=0

for all
i\in I
and all
k\geq j
by the assumption that the section belongs to
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
over each
U_{i}
. However, together these imply that
\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{T^{\prime}}=0
over
T^{\prime}
as well for each
k\geq j
, so we see that the tuple is
\mathcal{L}|_{T}
–lowered, satisfying the first requirement.

To see the second property, we again use the fact that since

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})=(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1}).

we have that

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U_{i}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{k_{i}}|_{U_{i}})=(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+2-k_{i}}|_{U_{i}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{\ell+1}|_{U_{i}})

for each
i\in I
. Now, because
(\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{U_{i}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{k_{i}}|_{U_{i}})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(U_{i})
by assumption, we have that

\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{U_{i}}=\left(\bigoplus_{a\neq\ell+2-j}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{a}|_{U_{i}}\right)^{\perp_{h}}

Since this holds for all
i\in I
, we conclude that

\mathcal{V}_{j}=\left(\bigoplus_{a\neq\ell+2-j}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{a}\right)^{\perp_{h}}

which shows that
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}(T)
as desired, and we are done.
∎

Example 4.21
.

Consider the split hermitian form
(S\sqcup S\to S,(\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})
of (
7
). Then,
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}
and
\mathcal{L}
–submodules which are direct summands of
\mathcal{O}^{d}\times\mathcal{O}^{d}
are of the form
\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{W}
for direct summands
\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
. Given a section

(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\times\mathcal{W}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}(S)

we may localize it sufficiently such that each
\mathcal{V}_{j}
and
\mathcal{W}_{j}
are constant rank
\mathcal{O}
–modules and therefore the tuple is both lowered and raised, meaning that

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\times\mathcal{W}_{\ell+1})=(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\times\mathcal{W}_{\ell+1})

(though it technically lands in a different sheaf). Additionally, the condition that the tuple is
\mathcal{L}
–lowered means that each
\mathcal{V}_{i}
and
\mathcal{W}_{i}
must have positive rank, i.e., they are non-zero. Now, for such a fixed tuple we may analyze the symmetry condition more easily. It states that

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{i}\times\mathcal{W}_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(\bigoplus_{j\neq\ell+2-i}\mathcal{V}_{j}\times\mathcal{W}_{j}\right)^{\perp_{h}}

\displaystyle=\left(\bigoplus_{j\neq\ell-2-i}\mathcal{W}_{j}\right)^{\perp}\times\left(\bigoplus_{j\neq\ell-2-i}\mathcal{V}_{j}\right)^{\perp}

and so
\mathcal{W}_{i}=(\oplus_{j\neq\ell-2-i}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp}
. This condition also implies that
(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-i}\mathcal{W}_{j})^{\perp}=\mathcal{V}_{i}
, so we only need to consider the first condition. Hence, sufficiently locally sections of
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}
take the form

(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp},\mathcal{V}_{2}\times(\oplus_{j\neq\ell}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\times(\oplus_{j\neq 1}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp}).

Additionally, if we consider the projection onto the first factor, we obtain a section

(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}

since
\rank(\mathcal{V}_{i})\geq 1
for each
i
. Due to the fact we have been working locally, what we have shown is that we have a map

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\times(\oplus_{j\neq 1}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp})

which is clearly injective and which is locally surjective. Thus, it satisfies the universal property of sheafification and induces an isomorphism of sheaves

\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}.

We warn that the same formula appearing above for constant rank direct sum decompositions does not apply to describe this isomorphism of sheaves. For example, over a disconnected scheme
S=X\sqcup Y
where
\mathcal{O}^{d}=(\mathcal{O}|_{X}^{d},\mathcal{O}|_{Y}^{d})
, if one naively applies the formula to a section

((\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{W}_{1}),(\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{W}_{2}),(0,\mathcal{W}_{3}))\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}

(where the
\mathcal{V}_{i}
and
\mathcal{W}_{j}
are constant rank) one obtains

((\mathcal{V}_{1}\times 0,\mathcal{W}_{1}\times(\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{2})^{\perp}),(\mathcal{V}_{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp},\mathcal{W}_{2}\times(\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3})^{\perp}),(0\times\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp},\mathcal{W}_{3}\times(\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3})^{\perp})

which is not
\mathcal{L}
–lowered since
\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}|_{X}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times 0)=0
. The correct image of this section is

((\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\perp},\mathcal{W}_{1}\times(\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{2})^{\perp}),(\mathcal{V}_{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\perp},\mathcal{W}_{2}\times(\mathcal{W}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3})^{\perp}),(0,\mathcal{W}_{3}\times(\mathcal{W}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{3})^{\perp}).

This can be corrected by using
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
of (
23
). In particular, the isomorphism of sheaves is

(25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{\mathcal{O}^{d}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathcal{V}_{1}\times(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+1}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\prime})^{\perp},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}\times(\oplus_{j\neq 1}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\prime})^{\perp})

where
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})=(\mathcal{V}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}^{\prime})
.

\blacksquare

The goal is to use the partial sums of sections in
\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
to build sections of
\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}
. When taking these partial sums we see the relationship between
\mathcal{L}
–subrank and
\mathcal{L}
–gap in our application.

Lemma 4.22
.

Let
L\to S
be an étale cover of schemes of degree
k
with corresponding commutative
\mathcal{O}
–algebra
\mathcal{L}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Rings
. Let
\mathcal{M}\colon\Sch_{S}\to\Ab
be a finite locally free
\mathcal{L}
–module of constant rank
d
. Consider two
\mathcal{L}
–submodules which are locally direct summands
\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{M}
and such that
\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{W}=0
, so that
\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{M}
. Then,

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W})=\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{W}).

Proof.

Since both the
\mathcal{L}
–gap and the
\mathcal{L}
–subrank are locally constant sections, it is sufficient to show the equality holds locally. Thus, we may assume that
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}^{k}
,
\mathcal{M}=(\mathcal{O}^{d})^{k}
, and that

\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{V}_{k}\text{ and }\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{W}_{k}

for
\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{W}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{d}
direct summands. In particular, this means that

\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W}=(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{1})\times\ldots\times(\mathcal{V}_{k}\oplus\mathcal{W}_{k}).

Then, setting
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{V}_{i})=r_{i}
and
\rank_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{W}_{i})=s_{i}
we can simply compute that

\displaystyle\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V}\oplus\mathcal{W})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(\{(r_{i}+s_{i})-r_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min(\{s_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq k\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{W})

to conclude the proof.
∎

Now we can see that the partial sum construction works as desired.

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
(f\colon L\to S,\mathcal{H},h)
be a regular Hermitian form of constant rank
d
. We have a well-defined map of sheaves

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{(\mathcal{H},h)}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell}\subseteq f_{*}(\mathcal{H})).

In particular, an
\mathcal{L}
–lowered tuple produces a symmetric
\mathcal{L}
–lowered flag. Furthermore, this is a surjective map of sheaves.

Proof.

We begin by checking that the resulting flag is
\mathcal{L}
–lowered. Since each component of a tuple
(\mathcal{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1})
is an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule, each component of the flag will be an
\mathcal{L}
–submodule. Now, assume that for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
and for some index
j
, we have

\mathrm{gap}_{\mathcal{L}|_{T}}\big((\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j})|_{T}\subseteq(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j+1})|_{T}\big)=0.

Using
4.22
, this means that

\mathrm{subrank}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{V}_{j+1}|_{T})=0

and therefore the condition that the starting tuple was lowered imposes that
\mathcal{V}_{k}|_{T}=0
for all
k\geq j+1
. Then, since
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}
, after restricting we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}|_{T}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{T}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1}|_{T}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{V}_{1}|_{T}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j}|_{T}

and so

(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j})|_{T}=\mathcal{E}|_{T}=(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{j+1})|_{T}.

Hence, we see that the resulting flag is
\mathcal{L}
–lowered.

Now, we check that the flag has the necessary symmetry property. We may verify this sufficiently locally where the map takes the form

(26)

\mathcal{S}\hskip-1.72218pt\mathit{t\hskip-1.07639pti\hskip-1.29167pte\hskip-1.07639ptf\hskip-2.15277pte\hskip-0.86108ptl}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}\to\mathcal{F}\hskip-1.72218pt\ell\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{a\hskip-1.07639ptg}^{-}_{((\mathcal{O}^{d},\mathcal{O}^{d}),h_{d})}.

Further, we may assume that we are working with constant rank submodules and so using the description in
Example

4.21
the map appears as

(\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{i}\times(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-i}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp},\ldots)\mapsto(\ldots,\left(\oplus_{k=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{k}\right)\times\left(\oplus_{k=1}^{i}(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-k}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp}\right),\ldots)

where we have only written the
i^{\text{th}}
terms. For each summand within the term

\oplus_{k=1}^{i}(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-k}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp},

we have inclusions
(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-k}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp}\subseteq(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\ell+1-i})^{\perp}
and so

\oplus_{k=1}^{i}(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-k}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp}\subseteq(\oplus_{m=1}^{\ell+1-i}\mathcal{V}_{m})^{\perp}.

Now by considering ranks, namely that
\rank((\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-k}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp})=\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell+2-k})
and

\rank((\oplus_{m=1}^{\ell+1-i}\mathcal{V}_{m})^{\perp})=\sum_{k=1}^{i}\rank(\mathcal{V}_{\ell+2-k})

we see that we have an equality

\oplus_{k=1}^{i}(\oplus_{j\neq\ell+2-k}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{\perp}=(\oplus_{m=1}^{\ell+1-i}\mathcal{V}_{m})^{\perp}.

Therefore, when we apply the perpendicular operator
\perp_{h_{d}}
to the image flag, the new
i^{\text{th}}
term will be the perpendicular of the
(\ell+1-i)^{\text{th}}
term which is

\displaystyle\left((\oplus_{k=1}^{\ell+1-i}\mathcal{V}_{k})\times(\oplus_{m=1}^{\ell+1-(\ell+1-i)}\mathcal{V}_{m})^{\perp}\right)^{\perp_{h_{d}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((\oplus_{m=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{m})^{\perp})^{\perp}\times(\oplus_{k=1}^{\ell+1-i}\mathcal{V}_{k})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\oplus_{k=1}^{i}\mathcal{V}_{k})\times(\oplus_{m=1}^{\ell+1-i}\mathcal{V}_{m})^{\perp}

which is the
i^{\text{th}}
term of the original flag. Hence, we see that the resulting flag is symmetric locally and thus it is also symmetric globally as required.

Finally, to see surjectivity we note that sufficiently locally when our map takes the form (
26
